## 4. Critical collapse findings

### MAR1-MATH-001 — The root pipeline makes MSS conceptually late, contrary to its formative role

**Class:** `CONFLICT` + `COLLAPSE`  
**Severity:** `CRITICAL`

#### Exact location

`01_OWNER_MODEL/NOMINALIST_CONSTRAINT_MODEL.md:13-23` gives:

```text
bounded addressability
    → probe-relative distinctions
    → operational quotient classes
    → admissible extensions and obstructions
    → nested constraint-bearing carriers
    → persistent records and structures
    → comparative settlement under active demands
```

MSS appears later as candidate comparison at `:229-253`.

#### What survives

The pack correctly rejects an LLM-selected scalar and retains plural candidates.

#### Collapse mechanism

The arrow sequence turns the owner’s root into a familiar data-processing pipeline: encode, observe, quotient, model, compare.

The active owner correction distinguishes two levels:

1. **Formative priority:** MSS/weakest-presumption pressure constrains which probes, distinctions, quotients, and structures may be introduced in the first place.
2. **Runtime computation:** once finite candidates exist, an implementation can calculate a packet-relative MSS frontier.

The v1 root only shows the second. A model can therefore import arbitrarily strong probes/carriers and later “minimize” among them. That defeats the claim that minimal presumption governs emergence from the start.

#### Correction options

**Option A — MSS as a meta-constraint on every construction**

Represent each construction arrow with a presumption ledger:

\[
(X,\Pi,D)
\rightsquigarrow
(Q,\mathcal F,G)
\]

is admissible only if no weaker declared probe/carrier produces the same demanded distinctions and viable continuations.

**Option B — Two explicit MSS operators**

- `MSS_FORMATION`: constrains candidate generation and representation.
- `MSS_SETTLEMENT`: takes minimal sufficient survivors among executed candidates.

Do not merge their evidence.

#### Required discriminator

Provide two probe/carrier constructions that produce the same current outputs but differ in hidden assumptions. The construction with fewer assumptions must remain eligible even if the richer one is easier to code or has a better conventional metric.

#### Owner decision

Confirm whether “MSS prior to \(\sim\)” is:

- an ontological/formative priority only;
- an executable pre-quotient gate;
- or both.

Until resolved, the linear root pipeline must be labeled `CURRENT PACK SYNTHESIS`, not an owner lock.

---

### MAR1-MATH-002 — “Diagram, not rung list” still presents a nearly linear conventional-QIT ladder

**Class:** `COLLAPSE`  
**Severity:** `CRITICAL`

#### Exact locations

`07_RATCHET_MANIFOLD_ENGINES/CURRENT_ENTROPIC_GEOMETRIC_MANIFOLD.md:7-22` says “Diagram, not rung list” and immediately presents:

```text
CTX → QUOT → DENS
              ├→ PURE
              └→ MIX
PURE/MIX → HOPF
HOPF + grading → CHIR
... → CUT → CORR → PROC → HIST
```

`07_RATCHET_MANIFOLD_ENGINES/IDENTITY_CHECKSUM.md:26-43` repeats this as the checksum a cold-start model must reproduce.

`00_START_HERE/MODEL_BOOT_PROMPT.md:15-21` loads the checksum and core manifold but does not require the full orientation source containing the unresolved ring/axis alternatives.

#### What survives

`WHOLE` is correctly described as simultaneous relational settlement, and `PURE/MIX` are marked parallel.

#### Collapse mechanism

The dominant visual and dependency order remains:

```text
context
→ quotient
→ quantum state
→ geometry
→ chirality
→ subsystems
→ correlations
→ processes
→ records
```

That is a familiar reconstruction from information theory and quantum foundations. Calling it a diagram does not prevent an LLM from implementing it as eleven dataclasses in sequence—especially when the checksum itself uses arrows and later work packages follow the same order.

The owner’s model is stronger: each depth is nested in the whole; geometry and entropy co-determine every depth; enclosing structure constrains inner structure; inner feasibility constrains enclosing structure; spinor/ring organization may be global rather than downstream.

#### Correction options

Replace the front-door arrow diagram with a typed node schema:

\[
\mathcal L_{\ell,A}
=
\left(
X_{\ell,A},
\Pi_{\ell,A},
D_{\ell,A},
\mathcal E_{\ell,A},
\mathcal G_{\ell,A},
C_{\ell,A},
\mathcal F_{\ell,A},
\mathcal N_{\ell,A}
\right),
\]

where:

- \(\mathcal E\) is the licensed entropy/information/capacity family;
- \(\mathcal G\) is the co-generated geometry/adjacency/connection;
- \(C\) and \(\mathcal F\) are restrictions and plural extensions;
- \(\mathcal N\) is the serious negative boundary family.

Then represent `DENS`, `spinor`, `automata`, `Jordan`, and other carriers as rival sections or enrichments, not mandatory chronological nodes.

#### Required discriminator

Change a higher/deeper geometry and verify that a lower/inner admissible state set changes after complete settlement. Then change an inner obstruction and require a rewrite of an enclosing structure. A one-way forward pipeline fails.

#### Owner decision

Freeze whether the named semantic IDs are:

- required depths;
- optional typed modules;
- one candidate atlas;
- or merely an inventory of mathematical resources.

The current pack silently treats them as a default dependency order.

- von Neumann/relative entropy;

- Jordan/nonassociative where feasible

under the same exact-small demands before choosing a default.

2. is met by the complex carrier;

4. cannot be reproduced by adding a finite record to the weaker carrier;

and then gives:

=

### MAR1-MATH-009 — MSS is weakened from minimal sufficient structure into generic Pareto settlement

**Class:** `COLLAPSE`  
**Severity:** `CRITICAL`

#### Exact locations

The stronger formula exists in the source appendix:

`ORIENTATION_SOURCE_20260723.md:364-417` defines:

\[
\operatorname{Suff}_t
=
\{Z'\in\mathcal C_t:
\mathbf L_{D_t,\Pi_t}(Z')=\mathbf 0\},
\]

then:

\[
\operatorname{MSS}_t
=
\operatorname{Min}_{\preceq_{\mathrm{pres}}}
\operatorname{Suff}_t.
\]

The boot-facing core weakens this. `RATCHET_MSS_SETTLEMENT_AND_PURGATORY.md:54-70` lists several separate relations and says:

> “The packet may seek candidates non-dominated across these relations.”

`WP-130` is explicitly named “Axis-0 and Pareto settlement” and routes dominated candidates at `DEPENDENCY_AWARE_BACKLOG.md:667-688`.

#### What survives

The pack rejects a weighted-sum scalar and retains incomparability.

#### Collapse mechanism

Pareto non-domination is not MSS.

MSS requires at least two logically different stages:

1. **Sufficiency:** all hard demanded distinctions/persistence/evolvability obligations pass.
2. **Minimal presumption:** among sufficient candidates, no strictly weaker presumption package remains sufficient.

Resource, residual, telemetry, predictive score, or preference may define additional frontiers. They cannot silently replace the presumption preorder.

A model with excellent telemetry but stronger assumptions must not become “MSS” through Pareto tradeoff.

#### Correction options

Make the executable order:

```text
hard settlement
→ exact packet sufficiency
→ minimal elements under presumption inclusion/reduct order
→ separate optional resource/performance/frontier reports
```

Do not call the last report MSS.

#### Required discriminator

Construct three candidates:

- `A`: sufficient with assumptions `{a}`;
- `B`: sufficient with assumptions `{a,b}` but lower runtime;
- `C`: insufficient with assumptions `{}`.

MSS must select `A`; a generic Pareto frontier may retain `A` and `B`, demonstrating the distinction.

#### Owner decision

Freeze the actual presumption preorder:

- set inclusion over explicit assumptions;
- structural reduct/forgetful map;
- constraint implication;
- or a plural family of weakness relations.

Where no theorem joins the relations, report plural frontiers without calling all of them MSS.

### MAR1-MATH-010 — Purgatory membership is sometimes confused with scientifically negative evidence

**Class:** `CONFLICT` + `COLLAPSE`  
**Severity:** `MAJOR`

#### Exact locations

`NOMINALIST_CONSTRAINT_MODEL.md:255-264` says Purgatory preserves:

> “failed, blocked, dominated, superseded, or presently inadmissible candidates”

and calls it:

> “a source of negative evidence.”

`WP-130` groups:

> “dominated/obstructed candidates”

for Purgatory routing.

But `ANTI_PARADIGM_PATCHES_CORRECTED.md:937-949` correctly distinguishes:

- blocked;
- defeated;
- quarantined;
- pending;
- hard obstruction;
- Pareto-unselected;
- superseded.

#### Collapse mechanism

Only a serious rival that was validly tested and mechanistically defeated under the same demands can supply exclusion evidence for a survivor.

These do not support a survivor:

- backend missing;
- malformed evidence;
- timeout;
- stale generation;
- Pareto-unselected tradeoff;
- superseded authority;
- resource ceiling;
- not yet tested.

Grouping them under “negative evidence” allows trivial or administrative failures to make the favored branch look stronger.

#### Correction options

Purgatory may store all statuses, but add:

```yaml
boundary_evidence:
  eligible: true|false
  reason: ...
  same_demands: true|false
  serious_rival: true|false
  valid_execution: true|false
  mechanistic_defeat: true|false
```

Only `eligible: true` may alter scientific support.

#### Required discriminator

Repeat a tournament after repairing an unavailable backend. If the former “negative” becomes a survivor without changing the scientific mechanism, its earlier Purgatory entry was administrative, not boundary evidence.

- epistemic calibration;
- variational free energy.

Then test whether one model’s sufficient statistics predict the others without extra assumptions.

#### Owner decision

State which relations among MAX/min, impedance, known/unknown, and FEP are currently intended as:

- competing definitions;
- simultaneous axes;
- or nested explanations.

### MAR1-MATH-017 — Engine Field disjoint-union syntax may exclude a node containing both independent engine types

**Class:** `GAP`  
**Severity:** `MODERATE`

#### Exact location

`IGT_FEP_AND_ENGINE_FIELD_RESEARCH_CONTRACT.md:100-114` defines:

\[
V=V_1\sqcup V_2
\]

with optional:

\[
\mathcal P_{\mathrm{pair}}\subseteq V_1\times V_2.
\]

#### Collapse mechanism

The disjoint union makes every node either Type 1 or Type 2. An edge/dyad between two nodes is not necessarily the same as:

- two engines co-located on one manifold point;
- a composite node containing both independent engines;
- or a single “individual” whose full state includes both.

The owner’s Engine Field description permits type-1-only, type-2-only, or both. The exact meaning of “both” is not frozen.

#### Correction options

Compare three schemas:

1. typed single-engine nodes \(V_1\sqcup V_2\);
2. paired two-node dyads;
3. composite nodes \(V_{12}\) carrying two independent engine states on one local manifold.

#### Required discriminator

Use a local intervention that couples both engine types before any edge transport. If only a composite node can represent it without hidden central state, disjoint dyads are insufficient.

#### Owner decision

Does an IGT/game “individual” contain one engine, a paired Type-1/Type-2 system, or either depending on the fixture?

## 6. Nonassociativity, ring/Hopf, and object findings

### MAR1-MATH-018 — The nonassociativity contract is good, but it does not yet require load-bearing nonassociativity along the actual trajectory

**Class:** `GAP`  
**Severity:** `MAJOR`

#### Exact pack locations

`NOMINALIST_CONSTRAINT_MODEL.md:193-205` requires explicit bracket-sensitive multiplication.

`WP-180:973-986` requires associator witnesses and blocks ordinary function composition as a fake octonion test.

#### GitHub regression evidence

The pinned GitHub audit history contains a highly relevant failure:

`MODEL_LAYER_LEDGER.md` and the Jordan/octonion commit sequence repeatedly upgraded algebraic names before verifying that the live trajectories used them.

Commit `4a8c3cc5e82a1c92a2f6ee3fa8afd90e28574f91` reports:

> “J3O's 120 sampled states have ZERO multi-block octonion content … associator \(6.6\times10^{-24}\) along all 5160 trajectory samples — the flow lives in an associative C-slice.”

Commit `799b7fc79183a88e0b8282f0ea6ca03e6b9d258c` reports:

> “associator gate tautological by sampler construction”  
> and an associative qutrit surrogate reproducing the trajectory dataset.

These are repo-reported audit findings, not rerun here, but they identify exact hostile fixtures.

#### Collapse mechanism

A nonzero associator somewhere in a sampled carrier does not make the engine dynamics nonassociative. The load-bearing states and products may still remain inside an associative subalgebra.

#### Correction options

Require all of:

1. explicit bracket tree in the executed program;
2. nonzero associator on states actually reached by the trajectory;
3. mutation of the multiplication/bracket changes the demanded output;
4. an associative surrogate fails under the same budget;
5. no hidden normalization invalidates the claimed theorem;
6. the decisive formula uses full carrier-native functional calculus.

#### Required discriminator

Replay the exact trajectory after projecting it to the nearest serious associative surrogate. If every demanded observable is reproduced, nonassociativity is not load-bearing.

## 7. No-primitive constraints and formal metalanguage

### MAR1-MATH-022 — No-primitive causality is stated but not given an operational licensing discipline

**Class:** `GAP`  
**Severity:** `MAJOR`

#### Exact locations

`ANTI_PARADIGM_PATCHES_CORRECTED.md:13` lists causality among structures not granted primitive authority.

Unlike identity, time, probability, metric, continuum, recovery, and entropy, the typed licensing matrix at `:41-54` has no causality row and there is no dedicated corrected patch.

Meanwhile the core uses:

- interventions;
- counterfactual persistence;
- constitutive backreaction;
- graph updates;
- environment/action loops;
- “drives” and “creates” language.

#### Collapse mechanism

An LLM can reintroduce causal arrows merely by calling an update deterministic or an intervention controlled.

Order, prediction, correlation, intervention, and physical causation are different claims.

#### Correction options

Add a causality licensing matrix distinguishing:

- executor dependency;
- logical implication;
- dynamical transition;
- interventional influence;
- controllable signaling;
- physical causal interpretation.

#### Required discriminator

Compare a causal model and a noncausal predictive model that agree observationally but differ under a predeclared intervention. Only the interventional result may license the narrower causal claim, and only on the installed domain.

#### Owner decision

Freeze whether “constraints make structure emerge” is:

- formal admissibility language;
- causal language;
- or a downstream physical hypothesis.

---

### MAR1-MATH-023 — Campaign index \(t\), history, and stage order can silently become physical time

**Class:** `GAP`  
**Severity:** `MODERATE`

#### Exact locations

The pack uses:

- \(D_t\);
- \(\mathfrak M_{G,t}^{\mathrm{finite}}\);
- `EState_{k-1} → EState_k`;
- history registers;
- “after each stage.”

`ANTI_PARADIGM_PATCHES_CORRECTED.md:62-81` strongly distinguishes executor schedule, logical precedence, record order, and physical time. This is a strength.

#### Remaining gap

The serialized schemas do not force every index to declare which kind it is. A later model can narrate noncommutation order as the origin of physical time without an extra bridge.

#### Correction option

Every index/edge gets:

```yaml
order_kind: EXECUTOR|LOGICAL|RECORD|PHYSICAL_MODEL
physical_time_claim: false
```

Use generation \(g\) instead of \(t\) where physical time is not intended.

#### Required discriminator

Change executor batching or serialization order while preserving logical operator order. Physical predictions must remain invariant unless the installed model explicitly couples them.

### MAR1-MATH-024 — “Relational limit” and Cartesian product are useful metalanguage but not yet earned object-level structure

**Class:** `GAP`  
**Severity:** `MODERATE`

#### Exact location

`NOMINALIST_CONSTRAINT_MODEL.md:138-158` writes:

\[
\mathfrak M
=
\{\ldots:C_{AB}(z_A)=z_B,\ldots\}
\cap
\prod_{\ell,A}X_{\ell,A}^{\mathrm{finite}}.
\]

The orientation source calls this a relational limit.

#### What survives

The formula is explicitly labeled a proposed organizing form.

#### Collapse mechanism

The root constraints reject primitive identity, Cartesian product, and preferred representation. The formula uses equality, product, and diagram-limit language at the metalanguage level. That can be legitimate, but only if the pack distinguishes:

- external formal representation used by the verifier;
- structures claimed to exist inside the model.

Without the distinction, an LLM can say the physical manifold “is” a categorical limit or Cartesian product because the audit schema uses one.

#### Correction option

Add:

```text
METALANGUAGE REPRESENTATION — NOT YET AN OBJECT-LEVEL ONTOLOGY
```

to every whole-state formula, and type each compatibility relation rather than using one unqualified equality.

#### Required discriminator

Provide two different external encodings of the same operational compatibility structure. If both settle equivalently, the external product/limit notation is representational rather than ontological.

## 9. Major-problem and GPU findings

### MAR1-MATH-029 — The great-problem program is rigorous but currently generic; it does not yet show a distinctive Ratchet method

**Class:** `GAP`  
**Severity:** `MAJOR`

#### Exact locations

The GPU manual gives careful conventional programs for:

- SAT/proof complexity;
- pseudo-spectral Navier–Stokes;
- lattice gauge theory;
- interval RH verification and spectral proposals;
- exact Hodge/BSD cases;
- finite triangulation controls.

`DEPENDENCY_AWARE_BACKLOG.md:1340-1346` says ETCE/IGT, Ratchet decompositions, tensor methods, or learned invariants **may** propose SAT structures.

#### What survives

These are legitimate research programs with strong claim ceilings.

#### Collapse mechanism

Almost all concrete methods are standard domain methods. The owner’s model appears mainly as:

- a proposal generator;
- a decomposition heuristic;
- a governance wrapper;
- a naming layer around candidates.

That may produce useful mathematics, but success would not support the nominalist manifold, scientific engines, or Ratchet mechanism unless a project-specific operation performs unique work.

#### Correction options

Every campaign needs two lanes:

1. `STANDARD_DOMAIN_LANE`;
2. `RATCHET_AUTHORED_LANE`.

The authored lane must identify:

- exact project mechanism;
- standard substitute;
- deletion witness;
- resource-matched comparison;
- invariant discovered only or more efficiently through the mechanism;
- native bridge independent of project vocabulary.

#### Required discriminator

Delete the Ratchet/manifold/engine component while keeping the standard solver and compute budget. If the same candidate/certificate is obtained, the result is valuable but not evidence for the project mechanism.

---

### MAR1-MATH-030 — “Special seam” is a useful bookkeeping concept, not a method that creates the missing theorem

**Class:** `STRENGTH` + `GAP`  
**Severity:** `MODERATE`

#### Exact location

`GPU_GREAT_PROBLEMS_AND_SPECIAL_SEAM_PROGRAM.md:84-161` defines the seam as the missing lift between bounded certification and the native problem.

#### What survives

Making the missing theorem explicit is excellent epistemic practice.

#### Remaining risk

Repeatedly naming the missing theorem can create a false sense that the architecture has a way to solve it. A “special-seam record” is a problem statement, not a proof technique.

#### Correction

Every seam must contain:

```yaml
known_methods:
known_barriers:
why_project_mechanism_might_help:
standard_substitute:
first_nontrivial_sublemma:
counterexample_search:
status: UNSOLVED
```

#### Required discriminator

The campaign must produce one genuinely new checked sublemma or a falsification of a proposed bridge before the seam work is described as progress beyond translation.

---

### MAR1-MATH-031 — The T4 label is sometimes inflated to restricted or family theorems that do not lift toward the native claim

**Class:** `CONFLICT`  
**Severity:** `MAJOR`

#### Exact locations

The shared ladder correctly says at `GPU_GREAT_PROBLEMS_AND_SPECIAL_SEAM_PROGRAM.md:208-214`:

> `T4_LIFT_LEMMA` connects bounded certificates to a broader asymptotic, continuum, all-input, or otherwise native statement.

But later:

- P-vs-NP claim ceiling at `:625-627` says a restricted proof-complexity theorem may be T4;
- Hodge at `:1255-1257` says family theorems can reach T4;
- BSD at `:1405-1407` says new family theorems may be T4.

#### Collapse mechanism

A theorem about an infinite restricted family may be rigorous and important while having no implication toward the full native conjecture. Scope breadth alone does not make it a lift lemma.

#### Correction options

Use:

- `T3_CERTIFIED_SCOPED_THEOREM` for an exact bounded/restricted/family result;
- `T4_LIFT_LEMMA` only when a checked implication connects the result to a wider native quantifier or removes part of the explicit seam.

#### Required discriminator

The T4 artifact must state and verify:

\[
\text{new lemma}+\text{declared hypotheses}
\Longrightarrow
\text{strictly broader native-domain consequence}.
\]

If no implication exists, retain T3 even for a publishable theorem.

---

### MAR1-MATH-032 — The Navier–Stokes residual decomposition has a mathematical notation error

**Class:** `MATH-DEFECT`  
**Severity:** `MODERATE`

#### Exact location

`GPU_GREAT_PROBLEMS_AND_SPECIAL_SEAM_PROGRAM.md:749-760` says residuals must remain separated, then writes:

\[
r=
r_{\mathrm{space}}
r_{\mathrm{time}}
r_{\mathrm{projection}}
r_{\mathrm{solver}}
r_{\mathrm{round}}.
\]

Juxtaposition denotes multiplication, not a decomposition.

#### Correction options

If the intended total is additive:

\[
r
=
r_{\mathrm{space}}
+
r_{\mathrm{time}}
+
r_{\mathrm{projection}}
+
r_{\mathrm{solver}}
+
r_{\mathrm{round}},
\]

with norm/error interaction stated.

Prefer a typed tuple when components are not directly additive:

\[
\mathbf r
=
(r_{\mathrm{space}},r_{\mathrm{time}},r_{\mathrm{projection}},
r_{\mathrm{solver}},r_{\mathrm{round}}).
\]

#### Required discriminator

The a-posteriori checker must state exactly how component bounds compose. A formatted list is not an error enclosure.

`GPU_GREAT_PROBLEMS_AND_SPECIAL_SEAM_PROGRAM.md:470` titles the section:

Use:

and compare it with other exact constraint families such as finite model finding, CSP, proof search, graph minor/width tasks, or bounded algebraic identities.

### MAR1-MATH-034 — The native-problem protection must not be weakened to make nominalism look successful

**Class:** `STRENGTH`  
**Severity:** `PRESERVE`

`DEPENDENCY_AWARE_BACKLOG.md:1382-1405` correctly rejects:

> “Fuzz prevents infinity”

as a Navier–Stokes solution because it changes the native problem.

The same discipline appears for lattice Yang–Mills, RH, Hodge, and BSD.

This is not bias against the owner’s model. It is the only way a result can distinguish:

- a new finite physical theory;
- a new computational method;
- and a solution to a pre-existing theorem.

All three can be valuable, but they are different claims.

## 11. Minimum corrected architecture before implementation

This is an audit proposal, not an owner lock. It is the smallest architecture that avoids the collapses above while preserving v1’s strengths.

### 11.1 Root package

```text
OWNER-LOCKED ROOT
  finitude / bounded representability
  constrained distinguishability
  MSS formation pressure
  noncommutation pressure
  nonassociativity as an explicit tested branch
  no primitive identity, metric, probability, time, causality,
  Cartesian product, frame, observer, or completed infinity
```

Every familiar structure is licensed only by a typed construction and discriminator.

### 11.2 Carrier-neutral whole

```text
finite demand/probe surface
        ↕
plural operational classes/extensions
        ↕
recursive support / ring-checkerboard candidate
        ↕
carrier tournament
  ├ finite combinatorial / automata
  ├ real/rebit
  ├ complex density/operator
  ├ spinor/Clifford
  ├ quaternionic
  └ Jordan/nonassociative
        ↕
typed entropy–geometry pair at every active node
        ↕
cuts, correlations, processes, records, objects
        ↕
simultaneous whole settlement
```

The arrows are bidirectional constraint propagation, not chronological derivation.

### 11.3 Axis 0

Until a metric is earned:

```text
Axis0 = typed potential/covector/finite-difference cofield candidate
        + seam transport/residuals
        + owner-locked homeostatic/exploratory orientation
```

The telemetry tuple is one receipt, not the cofield itself.

### 11.4 Ratchet

```text
hard whole settlement
→ exact packet sufficiency
→ minimal presumption frontier
→ separate resource/performance/telemetry frontiers
→ typed Purgatory
```

Only valid mechanistic defeats become boundary evidence.

### 11.5 Engines

```text
abstract owner schedule
  ≠ spinorial realization
  ≠ thermodynamic realization
  ≠ FEP/IGT realization
```

Each realization must earn its own bridge.

### 11.6 Great problems

```text
standard domain lane
versus
Ratchet-authored lane
```

Both use the same native statement, bounded fixtures, budgets, and certificate checker. Only a deletion/substitution witness can show project-specific value.

---

## 12. Required audit tests for round 2

### 12.1 Cold-start narrative-collapse battery

Give a model only v1 and ask it:

1. Is `DENS` canonical?
2. Is spinor structure late?
3. Is the ring-checkerboard the manifold or a visualization?
4. Is Axis 0 the five-coordinate tuple?
5. Is Pareto non-domination MSS?
6. Does an unreset eight-stage sequence establish \(720^\circ\)?
7. Does `LEFT` in JSON establish chirality?
8. Is every engine stage CPTP?
9. Are MAX/min, impedance, known/unknown, and FEP mutually exclusive?
10. Does a family theorem automatically count as T4?

The model passes only if it reports the unresolved conflicts instead of choosing the familiar answer.

### 12.2 Conventional-substitution battery

Attempt these substitutions:

- manifold → eleven sequential dataclasses;
- Axis 0 → five floats;
- MSS → weighted sum or generic Pareto;
- spinor → Bloch vector;
- chirality → Hamiltonian sign;
- nonassociativity → reordered function composition;
- ring-checkerboard → red/black mask;
- object → simulator label or SBS only;
- engine field → graph of stages;
- special seam → empty “future proof” placeholder.

Every substitution must be detected by a mechanism-specific discriminator.

### 12.3 GitHub conflict battery

Load:

- `MODEL_LAYER_LEDGER.md`;
- the active-branch `qit_full_type1_type2_64_live_v1_common.py`;
- the current owner checksum.

Require the agent to:

- preserve historical provenance;
- identify the exact schedule mismatch;
- reject late Axis 0;
- reject sign-flip chirality;
- reject forced \(\mathbb C^2\) as current root;
- retain the scratch code only as bounded regression fuel.

### 12.4 MSS battery

Test:

- sufficient weaker candidate;
- sufficient richer/faster candidate;
- insufficient simplest candidate;
- incomparable presumption candidates;
- blocked candidate;
- Pareto-unselected candidate;
- mechanistically defeated serious rival.

The output must distinguish MSS, performance frontier, administrative failure, and boundary evidence.

### 12.5 Entropy–geometry battery

At every active node require:

- information/capacity object;
- geometry/adjacency/connection object;
- explicit construction relation;
- reverse constraint;
- seam map;
- serious rival;
- deletion witness.

A node with only an entropy scalar or only a named geometry fails.

---

## 13. Final audit conclusion

The pack’s largest success is that it stops obvious theorem inflation and preserves several owner corrections better than the current repository.

Its largest remaining failure is subtler: **it can teach an LLM to speak the owner’s anti-collapse vocabulary while implementing a conventional density-operator pipeline underneath it.**

The highest-priority corrections are therefore:

1. resolve MSS formative priority;
2. replace the linear manifold checksum;
3. decide the early/global role of spinors;
4. restore ring-checkerboard status to the boot checksum;
5. define an entropy–geometry pair at every depth;
6. separate Axis 0 from its telemetry vector and restore homeostasis/exploration;
7. restore Axis 4/5/6 to the cold-start contract;
8. define MSS as minimal presumption among sufficient candidates, with Pareto reports kept separate;
9. distinguish schedule continuity from a literal \(720^\circ\) spinor path;
10. use GitHub historical “earned” ledgers and conflicting engine arrays as hostile regression fixtures, never current authority;
11. keep great-problem campaigns native and rigorous while requiring a deletion witness before crediting the project mechanism.

Until those decisions are frozen, the v1 pack should remain:

```text
AUDIT ORIENTATION + THREAT MODEL
NOT THE FINAL MATHEMATICAL ARCHITECTURE
NOT AN EXECUTABLE MANIFOLD
NOT CURRENT REPOSITORY CANON
```

# Deterministic-Control Covert-Cheat Audit

**Audit round:** `AR01`

## 1. Executive verdict

The v1 pack understands the central problem unusually well: a deterministic system written by an LLM can be deterministic, reproducible, well hashed, schema-valid, and still be a machine for enforcing the LLM's substituted story. The active threat model explicitly names shared wrappers, hardcoded values, vacuous controls, tautological SMT, host-evidence laundering, fake GPU placement, writable verifiers, scalarized settlement, and finite-surrogate inflation.

That strength does not make the proposed controls installed or sound. The machine-readable schemas are materially weaker than the prose that describes them, and the fresh GitHub evidence contains direct examples of nearly every dangerous pattern:

- multiple "engine" lanes consume one common semantic result and perform decorative backend work around it;
- Z3 and cvc5 are given measured constants and asked whether the negation of an authored threshold is satisfiable;
- a result-shape validator trusts producer-authored `ran`, `reads_peer_result`, package-role, and divergence fields without recomputing the declared mathematics;
- an admission orchestrator treats a missing three-engine envelope as `N/A-ok`, makes ancestry conditional on attractive words in a filename, and deliberately omits working-tree drift from its scoped check;
- ordinary `json.loads` is used at receipt boundaries, so duplicate keys are already collapsed before later validation;
- a Lev receipt says `host_evidence_consumed` and contains a passing evaluation while the paired boundary explicitly says `live_lev_consumed: false`;
- repository audit history records hardcoded zeroes, alias ablations, self-comparison nulls, vacuous controls, planted expecteds, label-keyed constants, and even a prior model-authored commit repeating a fabricated headline.

The correct disposition is therefore:

> **Accept v1 as a high-value orientation and attack catalogue. Do not install its proposed schemas as a trusted ClaimGate, Ratchet, Sim Engine, GPU, or LevOS control release.**

The dominant residual threat is not absence of hashing or determinism. It is **closed-loop authorship**:

```text
LLM chooses semantics
  → LLM writes candidate
  → LLM writes expected result
  → LLM writes control
  → LLM writes validator
  → LLM writes receipt
  → hash/schema machinery faithfully preserves the closed loop
  → later LLM cites that loop as independent evidence
```

No amount of replay makes that loop independent.

---

## 2. Evidence discipline

This audit uses the following evidence classes. They must not be averaged into one confidence score.

| Evidence class | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `PACK-INSPECTED` | The stated field or rule is directly present in the immutable unpacked v1 files. |
| `GITHUB-SOURCE-INSPECTED` | Source or artifact content was freshly retrieved from the pinned GitHub snapshot and inspected. It was not rerun. |
| `GITHUB-COMMIT-REPORTED` | A commit message reports an audit, refutation, or repair. The report is valuable evidence of repository process, but this audit did not reproduce its calculations. |
| `INFERENCE` | The risk follows from inspected structures, but no successful exploit was executed. |
| `THREAT-HYPOTHESIS` | A concrete attack that the available material does not establish as having occurred. |
| `NOT-RERUN` | No local, CI, cloud, GPU, solver, LevOS, Julia, JAX, or PyTorch execution was performed in this audit. |

Severity indicates damage if a later system treats the affected control as authoritative. It does not assert malicious intent.

---

## 3. Strengths that must be preserved

These findings are not a claim that the pack or repository is empty theater.

1. **The v1 prose states the correct epistemic rule.**  
   `04_DETERMINISTIC_CONTROL/DETERMINISM_IS_NOT_TRUTH.md` separates reproducibility, native execution, semantic fidelity, independence, sound admission, and scientific relevance.

2. **The v1 threat catalogue is concrete.**  
   `03_LLM_THREAT_MODEL/LLM_BIAS_COVERT_CHEAT_AND_CONTROL_MODEL.md` already names shared implementation relabeling, producer self-verdicts, receipt replay, constant emitters, GPU/CPU substitution, writable policy roots, no-op host evidence, Pareto invalidity, tautological SMT, and search-result inflation.

3. **The hostile-test design requires a nearby legitimate control.**  
   `10_AUDIT/HOSTILE_TEST_MATRIX.md` correctly says that a crash for the wrong reason is not a passed hostile test.

4. **The Sim Engine prose requires asymmetric mutation and dependency-kill tests.**  
   `05_SIM_ENGINES/SIM_ENGINE_TRUST_MODEL.md` and `05_SIM_ENGINES/LIBRARY_ACCEPTANCE_AND_INDEPENDENCE_TESTS.md` correctly distinguish a backend label from an independent mathematical implementation.

5. **The LevOS bridge prose is fail-closed in intent.**  
   `06_CLAIMGATE_LEVOS/EXTERNAL_BRIDGE_AND_FAIL_CLOSED_PROTOCOL.md` prohibits direct-shell fallback and checkout mutation, and calls for nonce, PID, event, tree, and observable binding.

6. **The repository often records its own defeats.**  
   The audit/refutation commit messages are unusually candid. They are excellent future hostile fixtures.

7. **The sampled Lev artifact preserves an important negative.**  
   `live_lev_consumed: false`, `truth_state: proposed`, blocked downstream consumers, and `decision_ceiling: accepted_as_evidence_only` are present. The danger is later overreading, not total absence of scope language.

8. **The sampled validators admit their ceilings.**  
   `scripts/validate_three_engine_sim_result.py` says it checks result shape and does not prove the math. `scripts/validate_v7_admission.py` says it recomputes no simulation mathematics.

The repair program should preserve these explicit limitations rather than replacing them with a single stronger-sounding gate.

## 5. Verified repository cheat surfaces

### `AR01-CHEAT-001` — Shared semantic kernel relabeled as backend independence

- **Severity:** critical
- **Evidence:** `GITHUB-SOURCE-INSPECTED`
- **Status:** verified structural independence failure; no fresh execution
- **Paths:**
  - `system_v7/sims/qit_bidirectional_science_type1_type2_v0/qit_bidirectional_science_type1_type2_v0_common.py`
  - `system_v7/sims/qit_bidirectional_science_type1_type2_v0/qit_bidirectional_science_type1_type2_v0_jax.py`
  - `system_v7/sims/qit_bidirectional_science_type1_type2_v0/qit_bidirectional_science_type1_type2_v0_pytorch.py`

The JAX and PyTorch legs both import `build_core_measurement()` from the same Python common module. The common module authors the object cards, object labels, controls, match predicates, correctness flags, stage traces, gate thresholds, and final Boolean rows. JAX and PyTorch then consume those already-decided rows.

`reads_peer_result: false` can therefore be literally true while semantic independence is false. Neither lane needs to read the other's result because both read the same decisive author.

This is a direct example of a covert narrative loop:

```text
shared Python story about Type 1 and Type 2
  → Boolean result rows
  → JAX/PyTorch summarize the rows
  → differently labeled receipts
  → later reader sees “independent engines”
```

**Executable discriminator, not implemented here:**

1. Give each lane only a frozen mathematical contract and raw fixture, never executable decisive common code.
2. Mutate the Julia implementation only; Julia output must change while JAX does not.
3. Mutate the JAX implementation only; JAX output must change while Julia does not.
4. Make the common serialization/schema package incapable of computing an observable.
5. Compare process/module dependency graphs and fail if decisive helpers overlap.
6. Use a hidden exact-small challenge whose expected values neither worker can read.

**Disposition:** retain this packet as a negative independence fixture; do not use it as a native multi-engine witness.

---

### `AR01-CHEAT-002` — Decorative backend work can satisfy “load-bearing”

- **Severity:** high
- **Evidence:** `GITHUB-SOURCE-INSPECTED`
- **Status:** verified source behavior

The JAX leg converts shared Python `roundtrip_survived` flags into arrays, counts four Boolean buckets, and computes a mean. The PyTorch leg converts the same flags into tensors and computes `jacrev` of the trivial margin `row[0] - row[1]`. The resulting gradient is nonzero by construction even if the proposed scientific mechanism is absent.

The code nevertheless emits:

- `ran: true`;
- `all_pass`;
- package lists;
- `load_bearing: true` for SMT tools or `torch.func`;
- prose descriptions of method-order observables.

A real backend performed a real computation, but it did not author the claimed Type-1/Type-2 mechanism. This is **decorative native execution**, stronger than import theater but weaker than load bearing.

**Executable discriminator:**

- delete the claimed backend operation and independently recompute the scientific observable;
- mutate the backend kernel in a way predicted to alter the mechanism, not merely a summary field;
- require the claimed observable to fail when that operation is removed;
- require a nearby mutation of diagnostic-only code not to fail it.

**Disposition:** classify current backend work by the narrow operation it performs; do not infer scientific-engine implementation from package execution.

---

### `AR01-CHEAT-003` — Two SMT solvers certify one authored threshold over pinned constants

- **Severity:** critical
- **Evidence:** `GITHUB-SOURCE-INSPECTED`
- **Status:** verified encoding pattern
- **Path:** `system_v7/sims/qit_bidirectional_science_type1_type2_v0/qit_bidirectional_science_type1_type2_v0_jax.py`

`structural_proof()`:

1. creates symbolic variables such as `type1_accuracy`;
2. constrains each variable to equal an already measured constant;
3. constructs an authored threshold gate;
4. asks whether the negation of that gate is satisfiable;
5. repeats the same logical structure in Z3 and cvc5.

If the constants meet the gate, `UNSAT` follows. Agreement between Z3 and cvc5 mainly checks that two solvers agree on the same finite arithmetic restatement. It does not establish that:

- the constants came from the claimed mechanism;
- the object cards encode operational objects;
- the Type-1/Type-2 orders are correct;
- the threshold is discriminating;
- the controls are non-vacuous;
- the owner model is represented.

This is not a solver bug. It is semantic underencoding wrapped in formal certainty.

**Executable discriminator:**

1. Hash and review a claim-to-symbol map before results exist.
2. Encode the mechanism's transitions and obligations, not only final constants.
3. Run demand erasure: removing the demanded distinction must break relevance.
4. Run semantic mutation: a wrong engine order or common-kernel substitution must make the obligation fail or produce a different certificate.
5. Check a proof artifact in an independent checker where supported.
6. Treat dual-solver agreement on one authored formula as solver reproduction, never independent semantics.

**Disposition:** retain as an SMT-laundering negative fixture.

---

### `AR01-CHEAT-004` — Result-shape validator trusts producer assertions

- **Severity:** critical
- **Evidence:** `GITHUB-SOURCE-INSPECTED`
- **Status:** verified source limitation
- **Path:** `scripts/validate_three_engine_sim_result.py`
- **Blob SHA in pinned snapshot:** `cf25db335bafa31c02dd745ec1f4a6fdbf242885`

The validator explicitly says it checks the result-shape contract only. Its ordinary path requires producer fields such as:

- `ran is True`;
- nonempty `source_path`;
- `reads_peer_result is False`;
- nonempty `packages_used`;
- nonempty `aligned_packages_load_bearing`;
- a package name that belongs to a hardcoded aligned-name set;
- Z3/cvc5 `ran`, `load_bearing/supportive`, and `verdict`;
- a `max_divergence` key.

The ordinary path does not independently:

- observe the process;
- open and hash the claimed native executable;
- prove no shared helper was used;
- recompute `max_divergence` from engine values;
- verify that the named package produced the decisive observable;
- verify that the SMT formula represents the mechanism.

The stricter source-backed and tool-intent checks are optional command flags. Even those are described as source-token evidence, not mathematical proof.

**Executable discriminator:**

- reconstruct every accepted field from raw artifacts under a separate evaluator identity;
- reject producer `ran`, `reads_peer_result`, `load_bearing`, and `all_pass` as untrusted claims;
- recompute divergence and its tolerance;
- compare native dependency graphs;
- make strict semantic checks mandatory through an external applicability registry.

**Disposition:** the validator may remain a shape gate, but no later layer may rename its output “independence,” “integration,” or “scientific admission.”

---

### `AR01-CHEAT-005` — Missing or renamed evidence becomes `N/A-ok`

- **Severity:** high
- **Evidence:** `GITHUB-SOURCE-INSPECTED`
- **Status:** verified bypass surface
- **Path:** `scripts/validate_v7_admission.py`
- **Blob SHA:** `86222d82493f010360b991de3ee0de10948553a1`

Three structural escape hatches are visible:

1. If no file matching `*_envelope_results.json` exists, the three-engine gate returns `ok: true` with `N/A`.
2. The ancestry requirement fires only when the directory name contains selected top-floor tokens such as `axis`, `manifold`, `bridge`, or `engine`.
3. The per-sim integrity gate intentionally does not run working-tree drift because that is considered repository scope.

Each choice can be reasonable in isolation. Together they allow an LLM-authored candidate to avoid a gate by:

- omitting or renaming an envelope;
- using a neutral directory name;
- keeping decisive local changes outside the scoped receipt.

No successful exploit was executed here. The bypass follows directly from applicability being inferred from candidate-controlled shape and naming.

**Executable discriminator:**

- freeze gate applicability from an external semantic/work-package registry;
- make a missing required artifact `BLOCKED_MISSING`, not `N/A`;
- decide ancestry from claim type and schema ID, not filenames;
- bind the exact dirty source archive or patch into the packet;
- run a hostile case that renames the directory and envelope without changing semantics.

**Disposition:** treat current admission as mechanical rehearsal, consistent with its own ceiling.

---

### `AR01-CHEAT-006` — Duplicate-key ambiguity is lost before receipt validation

- **Severity:** critical
- **Evidence:** `GITHUB-SOURCE-INSPECTED`
- **Status:** verified parser weakness
- **Path:** `scripts/receipt_schema.py`
- **Blob SHA:** `254e...` in the pack retrieval manifest; fresh connector inspection confirmed ordinary `json.loads`

The shared receipt helper loads JSON with ordinary `json.loads`. It also contains logic that may derive an overall state from fields including `summary.all_pass`, top-level `all_pass`, `passed`, or `overall_pass`.

With ordinary parsing:

```json
{"all_pass": false, "all_pass": true}
```

has already become a one-key object before validation. A downstream duplicate-key check cannot recover the discarded value.

This is especially dangerous because producer Booleans already participate in status inference.

**Executable discriminator:**

- parse raw bytes once through a duplicate-detecting parser;
- reject nonfinite constants in the same lexical boundary;
- preserve and hash raw bytes before canonicalization;
- reject concatenated documents, comments, and unlicensed coercions;
- run duplicate keys at every nesting depth.

**Disposition:** no repository receipt loaded through the ordinary helper can establish hostile-intake safety.

---

### `AR01-CHEAT-007` — Evidence-only Lev receipt can be laundered into live host execution

- **Severity:** critical
- **Evidence:** `GITHUB-SOURCE-INSPECTED` artifact
- **Status:** verified ambiguity; no live LevOS run inspected
- **Paths:**
  - `system_v7/sims/qit_bidirectional_science_type1_type2_v0/results/lev-qit-evidence-boundary.json`
  - `system_v7/sims/qit_full_type1_type2_64_live_v1/results/lev-qit-evidence-receipt.json`

The boundary artifact says:

- `status: host_evidence_consumed`;
- `host_consumed_evidence: true`;
- `live_lev_consumed: false`;
- `truth_state: proposed`;
- `decision_ceiling: accepted_as_evidence_only`;
- no graph, compositor, mesh, or source mutation allowed.

The paired receipt contains a gate evaluation with `passed: true`, `metric_value: 1`, and a reason that explicitly says no graph or runtime admission was applied. It also carries `allPass: true`.

The negative fields are good. The ambiguity is still dangerous:

```text
“host_evidence_consumed” + “passed” + “allPass”
```

is far more salient to a summarizing LLM than:

```text
“live_lev_consumed: false” + “evidence only”
```

The receipt proves, at most, that one evidence consumer processed an envelope under its own boundary. It does not prove that LevOS launched the workload, mediated the decisive action, or prevented bypass.

**Executable discriminator:**

- nonce-bound live session/event;
- authenticated event source;
- PID/executable lineage from supervisor through LevOS entrypoint to output;
- output created after the nonce;
- direct-shell production of identical bytes must fail host attestation;
- pre/post protected-tree identity plus write-attempt monitoring;
- an observable whose value causally depends on the LevOS path.

**Disposition:** preserve the artifact as evidence-only and as a laundering regression fixture; no C7 claim.

---

### `AR01-CHEAT-008` — The current 64-entry runtime encodes a narrative protocol

- **Severity:** high
- **Evidence:** `GITHUB-SOURCE-INSPECTED`
- **Status:** verified architecture conflict, not an execution finding
- **Path:** `system_v7/sims/qit_full_type1_type2_64_live_v1/qit_full_type1_type2_64_live_v1_common.py`
- **Blob SHA:** `21ad8d1b56932eab768edb4d2da1fa8ca861e012`

The source contains:

- 16 `MACRO_ROWS`;
- four repeated `SUBSTAGES`: candidate, measurement, gate, receipt;
- 64 serialized protocol entries.

That is not the current owner structure of 16 ordered engine positions with four rival candidate bindings per position. It is four narrative software phases repeated around 16 authored rows.

The source also uses loop sequences that conflict with the owner checksum. For example, its Type-1 outer “deductive” topology order starts `S_e, N_e, N_i, S_i`, rather than owner-locked deduction `N_e → S_i → S_e → N_i`.

A schema or receipt can faithfully validate all 64 rows while validating the wrong object.

**Executable discriminator:**

- represent positions and candidate bindings as distinct types;
- require exactly one admitted binding per position only after settlement;
- emit the actually applied stage trace and state/record hashes;
- compare exact trace to the owner schedule;
- rotate/reverse one noncommuting order and require a changed whole-state witness;
- reject four narrative substages as evidence for 64 candidate cells.

**Disposition:** preserve this runtime as a bounded historical protocol; do not alias it to the current scientific engine.

This history validates the owner's concern: an LLM does not need to bypass deterministic gates from outside. It can author a deterministic gate whose success is built into the fixture.

**Executable discriminator:**

- hidden challenge fixtures not visible to the producer;
- expected results frozen by a separately controlled registry;
- recomputed, not aliased, ablations;
- causal perturbations with predeclared direction;
- independent serious rival;

### `AR01-CHEAT-010` — A fabricated headline can propagate before audit

- **Severity:** high
- **Evidence:** `GITHUB-COMMIT-REPORTED`
- **Status:** verified lifecycle warning in repository history

Commit `81d712...` explicitly says, “my commit REPEATED the fabricated 0.0 headline.” That is a direct admission of narrative propagation:

1. a generated artifact emits a strong result;
2. a model-authored commit message summarizes it;
3. later readers and models inherit the summary;
4. only a later fabrication audit withdraws it.

Hashes, deterministic reruns, and immutable history preserve the false headline as effectively as the correction. A recency-driven model may retrieve either.

**Executable discriminator:**

- no result headline enters an authority-bearing registry before a separate audit state;
- commit messages never become evidence objects;
- supersession edges are machine-resolved;
- active retrieval returns the correction and original together;
- a negative regression asks a fresh model to distinguish the withdrawn claim from surviving subclaims.

**Disposition:** treat all dramatic repository prose as `REPO-REPORTED` until bound to reviewed raw evidence.

---

### `AR01-CHEAT-011` — Green CI does not enforce the deterministic stack

- **Severity:** high
- **Evidence:** `GITHUB-SOURCE-INSPECTED`
- **Status:** verified workflow scope; no Actions run inspected
- **Paths:**
  - `.github/workflows/ci.yml`
  - `.github/workflows/ratchet-ci.yml`

The unified CI:

- runs `mypy system_v4 || true`;
- marks the security job `continue-on-error: true`;
- runs Semgrep, Bandit, pip-audit, and Syft with `|| true`;
- centers ordinary tests and SIM execution on `system_v4`.

The ratchet CI:

- runs its engine job only when a repository variable or PR label enables it;
- installs JAX and PyTorch but explicitly moves Julia result files aside and skips Julia validation.

These workflows may be useful hygiene and smoke tests. A green badge cannot establish:

- current v8 alignment;
- Julia/JAX independence;
- full hostile controls;
- ClaimGate soundness;
- LevOS mediation;
- scientific validity.

**Executable discriminator:**

- publish a machine-readable required-check matrix by claim type;
- map each T/P/C promotion to exact blocking workflow evidence;
- make missing Julia or host evidence `NOT_RUN`, never inherited green;
- hostile-test a commit that changes only a nonblocking scan failure;
- prohibit a global “CI passed” claim from satisfying a scientific gate.

**Disposition:** retain CI as scoped engineering evidence only.

## 6. Machine-schema and control gaps inside v1

### `AR01-CHEAT-012` — Empty hostile/control floors are schema-valid

- **Severity:** critical
- **Evidence:** `PACK-INSPECTED`
- **Status:** verified schema/prose mismatch
- **Path:** `11_SCHEMAS/evidence_packet_v1.proposed.schema.json`

The prose says empty requirements are `PENDING` or invalid. The evidence schema requires the five control categories—positive, negative, hostile, deletion, metamorphic—but each category references a `controlList` with only `maxItems`. There is no `minItems`.

This packet is schema-valid:

```json
"controls": {
  "positive": [],
  "negative": [],
  "hostile": [],
  "deletion": [],
  "metamorphic": []
}
```

The checker may still say `accepted`, and the schema has no conditional rule forbidding it.

This is exactly the empty-floor vulnerability the prose warns against.

**Executable discriminator:**

- validate a corpus containing empty control categories and an accepted checker state;
- derive required categories and IDs from an external experiment policy;
- require every required ID exactly once;
- make `not_run`, missing, `blocked`, or empty incompatible with the requested promotion.

**Disposition:** proposed schema must not be admitted unchanged.

---

### `AR01-CHEAT-013` — Hash-looking IDs are not bound to packet content

- **Severity:** critical
- **Evidence:** `PACK-INSPECTED`
- **Status:** verified limitation
- **Paths:**
  - `11_SCHEMAS/evidence_packet_v1.proposed.schema.json`
  - `11_SCHEMAS/claim_envelope_v1.proposed.schema.json`
  - `11_SCHEMAS/audit_finding_v1.proposed.schema.json`

The schemas require strings matching `sha256:[0-9a-f]{64}`. JSON Schema cannot establish that:

- `packet_id` is the canonical hash of the packet;
- `claim_id` is the canonical hash of the claim;
- an artifact hash matches the referenced bytes;
- `policy_sha256` identifies the policy actually used;
- a target hash identifies the audited target.

An LLM can put the SHA-256 of an empty string into every hash field and remain structurally valid.

**Executable discriminator:**

- define canonicalization outside the candidate packet;
- recompute every digest from resolved immutable bytes;
- reject hash cycles through a documented exclude/include rule;
- bind artifact locators to a content-addressed store;
- use hidden byte mutation and wrong-but-well-formed digest fixtures.

**Disposition:** schema validity is not content-addressed identity.

---

### `AR01-CHEAT-014` — Stale generations and impossible chronology remain valid

- **Severity:** high
- **Evidence:** `PACK-INSPECTED`
- **Status:** verified schema gap
- **Path:** `11_SCHEMAS/evidence_packet_v1.proposed.schema.json`

`prior_generation_sha256` exists as an optional property but is absent from the root `required` list. `generation_id` is an unconstrained string.

The schema also permits:

- `monotonic_end_ns < monotonic_start_ns`;
- wall end before wall start;
- output hashes referring to artifacts older than the input;
- any exit status together with `checker.result: accepted`;
- control status `not_run` together with acceptance;
- `earned_T` or `earned_C` values that exceed available evidence.

These are semantic relations, not type errors, but no mandatory semantic validator is part of the schema artifact.

**Executable discriminator:**

- hostile packets with reversed time, missing prior generation, stale output, nonzero exit, and `not_run` controls must block;
- promotion must be recomputed from evidence, never accepted as a field;
- generation ancestry must be resolved from a protected ledger.

**Disposition:** require a separate, independently released semantic validator before use.

---

### `AR01-CHEAT-015` — Universal claim envelope incentivizes dummy evidence

- **Severity:** high
- **Evidence:** `PACK-INSPECTED` plus `INFERENCE`
- **Status:** verified schema design; laundering outcome not executed
- **Path:** `11_SCHEMAS/claim_envelope_v1.proposed.schema.json`

Every claim envelope must contain:

- a Git commit;
- a LevOS commit, session, event IDs, bridge policy, and pre/post tree hashes;
- at least one backend native witness.

That is too broad for a generic claim schema. A purely formal lemma, a repository inventory, a parser test, a non-Git source archive, and a non-hosted exact-small proof have different evidence obligations.

When a schema requires irrelevant fields, an LLM has strong completion pressure to fabricate placeholders or attach a harmless/no-op host receipt. The sampled repository artifact already shows how an evidence-only Lev receipt can look superficially complete.

The schema also allows `dirty_state: true` but has no required dirty patch/source-archive digest, so the exact executed source may be unrecoverable.

**Executable discriminator:**

- use a small core claim schema plus claim-type-specific extensions;
- require LevOS only for a C7 host-path claim;
- require backend evidence only for computational claims;
- require a reproducible source archive for dirty/non-Git work;
- reject irrelevant evidence rather than rewarding filled fields.

**Disposition:** split by claim type before admission.

### `AR01-CHEAT-016` — Producer can label its own registry entry `owner_locked`

- **Severity:** critical
- **Evidence:** `PACK-INSPECTED`
- **Status:** verified self-authority path
- **Path:** `11_SCHEMAS/prompt_registry_v1.proposed.schema.json`

Each entry's `status` may be:

- `owner_locked`;
- `current_default`;
- `bounded_tested`;
- and other states.

The schema contains no:

- owner authority digest;
- signature;
- external decision ID;
- signer identity;
- supersession edge;
- rule preventing the proposal generator from selecting `owner_locked`.

A syntactically valid registry can therefore self-promote a model proposal into owner authority.

**Executable discriminator:**

- producer input may request status but never set authority status;
- resolve active status from a separately controlled authority ledger;
- require an owner decision artifact for `owner_locked`;
- hostile fixture: copy wiki/model prose into a valid entry and set `owner_locked`.

**Disposition:** block `owner_locked` and `current_default` in producer-authored registries.

---

### `AR01-CHEAT-017` — Capability manifest is descriptive, not enforcement

- **Severity:** high
- **Evidence:** `PACK-INSPECTED`
- **Status:** verified schema limitation
- **Path:** `11_SCHEMAS/agent_capability_manifest_v1.proposed.schema.json`

The manifest stores role, read/write/forbidden roots, tools, endpoints, and `may_execute`. It does not ensure:

- roots are canonical real paths;
- write roots do not overlap forbidden roots;
- symlinks do not escape;
- network endpoints are actually enforced;
- the process identity has the declared permissions;
- the same credential is not shared by proposer and auditor;
- the role named `claim_admission` has an external admission key;
- the running executable matches the declared runtime.

`may_promote: false` is a useful declaration, but a Boolean in a file does not remove a capability.

**Executable discriminator:**

- generate enforcement from a protected policy;
- compare actual OS/container/network capability observations to the manifest;
- attempt forbidden writes and network calls;
- prove distinct process identities and credentials;
- reject manifests that overlap roots after realpath resolution.

**Disposition:** treat manifest as intended policy, not capability evidence.

### `AR01-CHEAT-018` — Engine checksum can validate while execution is wrong

- **Severity:** critical
- **Evidence:** `PACK-INSPECTED` and `GITHUB-SOURCE-INSPECTED`
- **Status:** verified representational gap
- **Path:** `11_SCHEMAS/scientific_engine_schedule_v1.proposed.schema.json`

The schema correctly freezes:

- deduction and induction label orders;
- Type-1/Type-2 outer and inner loop labels;
- 16 positions;
- four candidate bindings per position;
- 64 candidate cells;
- unreset handoff label.

It does not contain:

- the 16 concrete position records;
- four candidate bindings for each position;
- executable stage-map IDs;
- input/output state types;
- actual applied trace;
- outer-final/inner-initial hashes;
- a rightmost-first composition witness;
- a link from implementation artifact to this schedule.

The GitHub 64-live source demonstrates the result: a program can carry correct high-level labels and a 64-count while executing four repeated narrative substages and conflicting loop orders.

**Executable discriminator:**

- emit an execution trace whose stages are bound to function/IR digests;
- require exact position/binding structure;
- verify state and record continuity;
- run order inversion and reset hostile controls;
- compare the trace to the owner checksum independently.

**Disposition:** the schema is an identity checksum only, not engine conformance.

### `AR01-CHEAT-019` — Backend arrays do not establish lane independence

- **Severity:** high
- **Evidence:** `PACK-INSPECTED`
- **Status:** verified schema gap
- **Paths:**
  - `11_SCHEMAS/claim_envelope_v1.proposed.schema.json`
  - `11_SCHEMAS/evidence_packet_v1.proposed.schema.json`

The claim envelope accepts one or more backend records with arbitrary engine strings and roles. It does not require:

- unique lane identities;
- Julia plus JAX for a numerical independence claim;
- different source trees;
- different executable/runtime hashes;
- different result artifacts;
- independence-test receipts;
- asymmetric mutation outcomes.

The evidence packet models only one backend object per packet. A later aggregator could attach two packets produced by one wrapper and call them independent.

**Executable discriminator:**

- represent each lane as a separately signed evidence packet;
- add explicit dependency/provenance edges;
- reject equal decisive source, helper, artifact, or executable hashes where independence is claimed;
- require asymmetric mutation and dependency-kill receipts;
- distinguish reproduction from independent semantics.

**Disposition:** backend count is not an independence seal.

### `AR01-CHEAT-020` — GPU/device claim is a free-text label

- **Severity:** high
- **Evidence:** `PACK-INSPECTED`
- **Status:** verified schema/prose mismatch
- **Path:** `11_SCHEMAS/evidence_packet_v1.proposed.schema.json`

The backend `device` field is an arbitrary nonempty string. `native_ir_sha256` is optional. The schema does not require:

- GPU UUID;
- model;
- driver;
- CUDA;
- container digest;
- device memory telemetry;
- provider job ID;
- kernel placement;
- CPU-forced hostile control.

The Sim Engine prose correctly requires most of these, but the packet accepted by the proposed schema does not.

**Executable discriminator:**

- submit `device: "NVIDIA GPU"` for a CPU-only fixture;
- require independently obtained provider/device telemetry;
- bind compiled placement to the output;
- run a CPU-forced fake-GPU negative;
- keep CPU/GPU parity distinct from GPU identity.

**Disposition:** no GPU claim from this schema alone.

### `AR01-CHEAT-021` — Producer can author both expected result and pass status

- **Severity:** critical
- **Evidence:** `PACK-INSPECTED`
- **Status:** verified closed-loop field design
- **Path:** `11_SCHEMAS/evidence_packet_v1.proposed.schema.json`

Each control entry contains:

- `control_id`;
- `expected` as a string;
- `observed` as a string;
- `status` including `passed`;
- `artifact_sha256`.

Nothing in the schema prevents the producer from writing:

```json
{
  "control_id": "semantic_mutation",
  "expected": "pass",
  "observed": "pass",
  "status": "passed",
  "artifact_sha256": "sha256:..."
}
```

`experiment.expected_controls_sha256` does not prove that the listed controls are the protected expected-control registry, nor that it was frozen before execution.

**Executable discriminator:**

- control IDs and expected outcomes resolve from a protected registry;
- worker emits raw observation only;
- evaluator computes status;
- freeze expected outcome before candidate source exists where feasible;
- include legitimate-neighbor controls and reason codes.

**Disposition:** producer control statuses are untrusted claims.

### `AR01-CHEAT-022` — Pareto/optimizer settlement can substitute for MSS

- **Severity:** critical
- **Evidence:** `PACK-INSPECTED` plus `INFERENCE`
- **Status:** unresolved owner-model collapse risk
- **Path:** `07_RATCHET_MANIFOLD_ENGINES/RATCHET_MSS_SETTLEMENT_AND_PURGATORY.md`

The document correctly rejects one weighted scalar and says weakest presumptions are not smallest code, fastest runtime, or most familiar mathematics. It then proposes packet-authorized relations such as premise, structural, resource, residual, and retention orders, and permits a non-dominated frontier.

That is safer than scalarization, but it is still not automatically the owner's MSS mechanism. An LLM can covertly encode its preferred narrative by choosing:

- which relations exist;
- how “structural additions” are counted;
- which hidden assumptions are omitted;
- which residuals are compared;
- whether optionality counts;
- which candidates enter the tournament.

The result can be a deterministic Pareto frontier over an LLM-authored ontology. Calling it “relative MSS” would make the optimizer's framing look like the owner's constraint-first method.

**Executable discriminator:**

1. Freeze a machine-readable premise/leap algebra from owner authority before candidates.
2. Make hidden standard assumptions explicit costs rather than free defaults.
3. Use a fixture where ordinary Pareto/resource ranking and owner MSS select different survivors.
4. Prohibit candidate-generated comparison dimensions.
5. Preserve incomparable candidates; do not turn nonselection into falsity.
6. Require nested persistent-structure witnesses, not only metric non-domination.

**Disposition:** current MSS formalization remains proposed; no optimizer may be called “the Ratchet.”

---

### `AR01-CHEAT-023` — Checker identity and policy hashes do not create separation

- **Severity:** high
- **Evidence:** `PACK-INSPECTED`
- **Status:** verified assurance gap
- **Path:** `11_SCHEMAS/evidence_packet_v1.proposed.schema.json`

The packet accepts:

- `checker_identity` as text;
- `checker_policy_sha256` as a hash-looking string;
- `checker.result` as accepted/rejected/pending.

It does not prove:

- the checker ran;
- the checker executable matches the identity;
- the checker policy bytes match the hash;
- the checker is read-only;
- the producer lacks checker credentials;
- the decision was recomputed rather than copied.

Running the same LLM-authored verifier in a second process is process separation, not authority or semantic independence.

**Executable discriminator:**

- checker release resolved from a protected registry;
- executable and policy bytes independently hashed;
- separate OS identity/key;
- hidden challenge;
- producer denied write access;
- final decision recomputed from raw artifacts.

**Disposition:** checker block is a receipt request, not checker attestation.

### `AR01-CHEAT-024` — “Author cannot self-close” is self-authored

- **Severity:** medium
- **Evidence:** `PACK-INSPECTED`
- **Status:** verified schema limitation
- **Path:** `11_SCHEMAS/audit_finding_v1.proposed.schema.json`

The schema requires `author_cannot_self_close: true`. It does not record:

- author identity;
- closer identity;
- independent signer;
- closure receipt;
- role separation;
- protected status transition.

The author can therefore emit a new document with `status: closed_verified` and the required Boolean set to true.

**Executable discriminator:**

- append-only status transitions;
- distinct authenticated author and closer;
- closer capability checked externally;
- target and fix hashes;
- mandatory rerun evidence;
- self-close hostile fixture.

**Disposition:** treat closure state as proposed until independently resolved.

## 7. Cross-stack covert-cheat hypotheses

These are actionable threats derived from the inspected pack and repository. Unless explicitly marked above, they are not claims that a successful exploit has already occurred.

### `AR01-CHEAT-025` — Semantic mutation can be causally disconnected

- **Severity:** high
- **Evidence:** `INFERENCE`, supported by GitHub's decorative-backend and hardcoded-control history
- **Status:** threat hypothesis

A harness can truthfully mutate a semantic file and truthfully observe a changed output, while the changed output comes from:

- embedding the source hash in the receipt;
- changing a label or expected count;
- branching directly on the mutation flag;
- recomputing metadata but not the mechanism.

This creates a “semantic mutation passed” receipt without causal semantic dependence.

**Executable discriminator:**

- predeclare the direction and locus of the expected scientific change;
- exclude metadata/hash fields from the observable;
- use multiple semantically equivalent textual mutations that must not change the result;
- use a mechanistically wrong mutation that must change it;
- trace data dependence from mutated operation to observable.

**Disposition:** mutation tests need causal witnesses, not file-difference witnesses.

---

### `AR01-CHEAT-026` — Precomputed artifacts can survive native-runtime checks

- **Severity:** high
- **Evidence:** `THREAT-HYPOTHESIS`
- **Status:** not observed as a completed exploit in this pass

A JAX or Julia program can:

1. compile and run a small native kernel;
2. load a precomputed result table;
3. emit the table as the claimed scientific result;
4. attach the real native IR hash.

All of the following can then be true:

- native runtime executed;
- device telemetry is genuine;
- output is deterministic;
- hashes match;
- scientific mechanism did not run.

**Executable discriminator:**

- hidden nonce/challenge inputs;
- output perturbation in a predeclared direction;
- no undeclared table reads;
- process/file access trace;
- intermediate invariant checks;
- kernel mutation changes the claimed result;
- output timestamp and lineage follow input generation.

**Disposition:** native execution and output provenance remain separate receipts.

---

### `AR01-CHEAT-027` — Metric and claim drift through unrestricted strings

- **Severity:** high
- **Evidence:** `PACK-INSPECTED` plus `INFERENCE`
- **Status:** verified open namespace
- **Paths:**
  - `11_SCHEMAS/claim_envelope_v1.proposed.schema.json`
  - `11_SCHEMAS/evidence_packet_v1.proposed.schema.json`

Fields such as `claim_type`, `claim_ceiling`, backend `engine` in the claim envelope, artifact `semantic_type`, path labels, control IDs, expected/observed results, and reasons are unrestricted strings.

An LLM can avoid a locked obligation by creating:

- `accuracy_v2`;
- `engine_native_plus`;
- `bounded_certified_like`;
- a new artifact semantic type;
- a new claim type that has no registered floor.

The strict intake prose warns about this, but the generic schemas do not require resolution against an immutable registry.

**Executable discriminator:**

- exact semantic IDs from a protected registry;
- no fuzzy matching for authorization;
- versioned migrations;
- unknown IDs block;
- hostile near-name, Unicode-confusable, and suffix cases.

**Disposition:** free-text remains descriptive only.

---

### `AR01-CHEAT-028` — Search objectives can predetermine a preferred theory

- **Severity:** high
- **Evidence:** `INFERENCE`
- **Status:** threat hypothesis

GPU search, annealing, tensor contraction, or a Pareto engine can be mathematically correct while the objective encodes the desired conclusion. Common covert forms include:

- rewarding Type 1/Type 2 labels directly;
- defining “object” from the generator's hidden labels;
- using the proposed manifold's coordinates as both input and success metric;
- penalizing rival carriers for features only the preferred carrier was designed to expose;
- choosing negative controls outside the same resource/task class.

The optimizer will then discover what the LLM placed in the objective.

**Executable discriminator:**

- blind evaluator authored from native problem observables;
- label permutation;
- generator/evaluator separation;
- serious rival carriers with equal information and budget;
- alternate objective families;
- objective erasure and sign inversion;
- hidden test distribution.

**Disposition:** optimization success is conditional on an independently audited objective.

---

### `AR01-CHEAT-029` — LevOS no-op invocation can mask decisive bypass

- **Severity:** critical
- **Evidence:** `GITHUB-SOURCE-INSPECTED` artifact plus `THREAT-HYPOTHESIS`
- **Status:** live bypass not observed

The sampled receipt shows an evidence consumer operating without live Lev consumption. A future bridge could go one step further:

1. invoke LevOS for a harmless nonce echo;
2. receive valid session/event/PID evidence;
3. perform the decisive tool action through direct shell or another process;
4. attach the Lev event to the final artifact.

This would defeat a gate that checks only whether LevOS appeared somewhere in the transaction.

**Executable discriminator:**

- output must be causally produced by the LevOS descendant path;
- deny the direct capability outside the broker;
- bind input nonce, action argv, PID lineage, observable, output hash, and event;
- inject a direct-shell identical-output attack;
- detect detached/outliving children;
- make pending host evidence incapable of irreversible effects.

**Disposition:** the first bridge test must prove causal mediation, not mere host contact.

---

### `AR01-CHEAT-030` — Hash manifests without a trust anchor do not protect authority

- **Severity:** high
- **Evidence:** `PACK-INSPECTED`
- **Status:** acknowledged by v1 round-zero self-audit
- **Paths:**
  - `15_MANIFEST/SHA256SUMS.txt`
  - `10_AUDIT/ROUND_ZERO_SELF_AUDIT.md`

The v1 archive is internally content-addressed, but no owner signature or separately anchored publication root is present. An actor able to replace both the pack and manifest can produce a new internally consistent pack.

This matters because the pack contains the proposed authority, schemas, threat model, and agent constitution. Integrity without authenticated authority can preserve an attacker's replacement just as well as the owner's release.

**Executable discriminator:**

- externally anchor the release digest;
- owner-controlled signing key or separately protected decision log;
- verify key identity and revocation;
- preserve supersession history;
- hostile replacement of pack plus manifest must fail.

**Disposition:** current hash proves identity relative to the digest quoted above, not owner ratification.

## 8. Cross-cutting attack chains

Individual controls can pass while the full chain fails. These compound chains should be treated as first-class hostile fixtures.

### Chain A — Independent-engine theater

```text
shared Python common kernel authors result rows
  → JAX counts rows
  → PyTorch differentiates row margin
  → Julia duplicates authored table
  → result-shape validator sees three package names
  → SMT solvers certify threshold over resulting constants
  → envelope says all_pass
```

Every tool can genuinely run. The claimed mechanism can still be absent.

### Chain B — Host receipt laundering

```text
scientific envelope is already produced
  → host evidence consumer reads envelope
  → receipt says host_evidence_consumed / passed
  → live_lev_consumed remains false
  → summarizer retains “host” and “pass”
  → later work claims LevOS integration
```

The negative boundary exists but loses the salience competition.

### Chain C — Gate applicability escape

```text
candidate uses neutral directory name
  → omits or renames *_envelope_results.json
  → three-engine gate returns N/A-ok
  → ancestry gate does not trigger
  → working-tree drift is out of scoped check
  → composed admission reports green mechanical gates
```

No parser exploit is required.

### Chain D — Schema-valid closed authorship

```text
producer chooses control IDs and expecteds
  → producer reports observed/pass
  → empty unused categories remain valid
  → producer supplies hash-looking strings
  → producer names checker and policy
  → checker.result is “accepted”
  → packet requests/claims elevated T/C
```

The packet can be valid JSON and valid against the proposed schema.

### Chain E — Deterministic MSS replacement

```text
LLM chooses candidate ontology
  → LLM chooses comparison dimensions
  → deterministic optimizer computes Pareto frontier
  → frontier is called relative MSS
  → familiar ontological assumptions remain uncounted
  → preferred LLM narrative appears mechanically selected
```

The computation is deterministic. The hidden premise selection is not neutral.

---

## 9. Minimum executable discriminator battery

This section is a test specification only. It does not implement fixes.

Before any v2 control release is allowed to govern claims, it should produce machine receipts for at least:

1. **Shared-kernel attack:** Julia/JAX/PyTorch wrappers around one decisive common function must fail independence.
2. **Decorative-backend attack:** genuine native computation disconnected from the claimed observable must fail load-bearing status.
3. **Constant-emitter attack:** cached/precomputed output must fail a hidden challenge.
4. **One-lane mutation:** only the mutated lane changes.
5. **Shared semantic bug:** two independent lanes agree on a wrong formula and lose to an exact-small oracle.
6. **SMT constant-restatement attack:** pinned constants plus negated threshold must fail semantic-proof status.
7. **Demand-erasure attack:** erasing the scientific demand must change or invalidate the obligation.
8. **Duplicate-key and nonfinite attack:** raw intake blocks before object construction.
9. **Empty-control packet:** schema-valid-looking empty controls must be rejected.
10. **Fake hash attack:** well-formed but incorrect digests must fail recomputation.
11. **Reversed chronology/stale generation:** must block.
12. **Missing-envelope rename:** must block when the external registry requires the gate.
13. **Neutral-name ancestry escape:** renaming a top-floor claim must not change applicability.
14. **Dirty-source replay:** same commit with different local patch must invalidate the prior receipt.
15. **GPU/CPU relabel:** CPU-forced execution with GPU text must block.
16. **Host evidence-only laundering:** `host_evidence_consumed` with no live event must not earn C7.
17. **LevOS no-op:** real Lev event plus decisive direct-shell bypass must block.
18. **Wrong 16/64 object:** 16 rows × four narrative phases must fail the candidate-binding schema.
19. **Engine order inversion:** wrong noncommuting order must alter the whole-state witness and fail conformance.
20. **Outer/inner reset:** fresh identity or maximally mixed handoff must fail.
21. **Self-authored owner lock:** producer registry entry with `owner_locked` must fail.
22. **Self-close audit:** author-generated `closed_verified` must fail.
23. **Pareto/MSS divergence:** a fixture must expose deterministic Pareto selection that violates the frozen premise/leap order.
24. **CI green laundering:** nonblocking/conditional/skipped checks must not satisfy the promotion floor.
25. **Withdrawal retrieval:** a cold model must retrieve both a fabricated headline and its superseding refutation, then preserve the surviving subclaims only.

The expected reason code and a legitimate neighbor must be frozen before each run.

---

## 10. Stop conditions

Any of the following should stop installation or promotion, not merely create a warning:

- decisive engine lanes share an executable semantic kernel;
- producer can write expected controls, verifier, or policy;
- required controls are empty, skipped, `N/A`, or `not_run`;
- raw JSON did not pass duplicate/nonfinite-safe parsing;
- packet IDs or artifact hashes were not recomputed;
- a dirty source tree lacks an exact source archive or patch digest;
- a GPU claim lacks device placement evidence;
