| Julia | reference semantics | readable independent reference implementation; exact/rational/symbolic computation where suitable; named scientific libraries; certificate preparation or checking | the word “Julia” does not make a result authoritative or correct |
| JAX | numerical workhorse | CPU golden numerical runs in declared 64-bit numerical mode; batched/JIT computation; NVIDIA GPU search and simulation; differentiable numerical kernels | import, device allocation, or decorative array summaries do not prove the scientific arrow ran |
| NumPy | satellite support | transparent exact-small checks, conversion, inspection, diagnostics, and declared baselines | never the hidden common result constructor, silent fallback, main workhorse, or fake third independent engine |
| PyTorch | support and training | a named training, perception, graph, or autograd task after a task-specific justification and ablation | never mandatory merely to produce an engine count; never a substitute for Julia reference semantics or JAX workhorse execution |

The Codex-Ratchet scientific program, Sim Engine fleet, ClaimGate control plane, and LevOS host path remain distinct systems:

- Sim Engines compute declared maps.
- ClaimGate authorizes routes, validates evidence, detects bypass, and fails closed.
- The scientific Ratchet compares constrained branches and retains plural survivors under the owner-bound MSS discipline; it does not become a generic Pareto sorter.
- LevOS supplies a protected host-mediated execution path only when the claim or capability contract requires that path.
- An LLM or council may propose a task, implementation, repair, or update. It may not select its own evidence floor, certify its own execution, promote a dependency, settle MSS, or declare a scientific result admitted.

## 1. The object being controlled

The word “engine” is overloaded in the project. This design separates five typed objects.

| Object | Meaning | Example | Trust is attached to |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Scientific engine** | the owner's Type-1/Type-2 dual-loop information/entropy engine mechanics | Type 1 Left: outer Deduction, inner Induction | exact schedule, candidate bindings, state handoff, and tested maps |
| **Compute lane** | an independently executed implementation estate | Julia reference or JAX workhorse | executable, environment, source, native witness, and arrow |
| **Library capability** | one named API that can implement part of a map | a selected Julia optimization package or JAX ODE solver | one isolated acceptance contract, not package availability |
| **Arrow** | a typed mathematical transformation | a density carrier to a declared entropy observable | domain, codomain, maps, units, error relation, fixtures, and implementations |
| **Campaign run** | a bounded assembly of arrows under one scientific question | a finite manifold or engine-field fixture | frozen contract, selected arrow graph, run ticket, receipts, and claim ceiling |

No object inherits trust from another by name. A library can be accepted while an arrow using it is not integrated. An arrow can be integrated on exact-small fixtures while a campaign is not ready. A campaign can run while a theorem-level lift remains absent.

## 2. Owner-alignment constraints that the control plane must not rewrite

The fleet policy is subordinate to the owner model. It must carry, not smooth away, the following constraints and open conflicts from Audit Round 1.

### 2.1 Fixed current owner statements

- Julia is the reference lane.
- JAX is the numerical workhorse.
- NumPy is satellite support and is not the main workhorse.
- PyTorch is support/training where it earns that role.
- LevOS is a separate repository under separate development and must not be modified by the external ClaimGate bridge.
- ClaimGate is an external deterministic tool/control layer, distinct from both LevOS and the scientific Ratchet.
- LLMs and councils propose. They do not self-certify, settle MSS, or define scientific truth.
- Deduction order is `Ne → Si → Se → Ni`.
- Induction order is `Ne → Ni → Se → Si`.
- Type 1 Left uses outer Deduction and inner Induction.
- Type 2 Right uses outer Induction and inner Deduction.
- The outer-to-inner state and record handoff is unreset.
- “64” means 16 ordered engine positions with four rival candidate bindings at each position, not 64 chronological narrative substages.
- IGT has `WINwin`, `WINlose`, `LOSEwin`, and `LOSElose`; WIN/LOSE may not be moralized into good/bad.
- Heterogeneous entropies and geometry responses may not be collapsed into an untyped scalar soup.
- A bounded GPU computation is not a proof of an unrestricted mathematical or physical statement.

### 2.2 Open owner decisions that machinery may not decide by convenience

The fleet must preserve branch IDs for, at minimum:

- the exact nominalist/no-primitive root registry;
- whether operational probes precede MSS, follow MSS, or co-constrain it;
- the precise MSS comparator beyond the prohibition against replacing it with generic Pareto settlement;
- the non-linear topology of the entropic-geometric manifold;
- the native versus presentational role of the ring-checkerboard/nested-shell construction;
- where spinor, chirality, Weyl structure, and global orientation enter;
- the exact Axis-0 carrier and maps;
- quantitative bindings for Axes 4, 5, and 6;
- specific mathematical bindings of `Ne`, `Si`, `Se`, and `Ni`;
- the concrete v8 content manifest.

ClaimGate must reject a run that silently resolves one of these by choosing the easiest available library or the most conventional mathematical representation.

### 2.3 Audit defects this design explicitly answers

This design addresses, but does not claim to have repaired, the following Round-1 blockers:

- `M-18`: shared semantic kernels and decorative backend work;
- `M-19`: formal validators that restate authored thresholds;
- `M-20`: weak parser, receipt, chronology, and content binding;
- `M-21`: universal capability fields that incentivize dummy evidence;
- `M-22`: fail-open or no-op LevOS use;
- `M-26`: disputed ordering between Sim Engine independence and control-plane integration;
- `M-27`: conditional CI and skipped execution;
- `M-28`: strong audits not mechanically connected to quarantine;
- the philosophy conflicts in `M-01` through `M-17`;
- the native-problem and GPU claim ceiling in `M-25`.

## 3. Threat model: how an LLM will make the fleet look healthier than it is

The primary threat is not simply false prose. It is a valid-looking execution topology whose decisive work bypasses the claimed system.

### 3.1 Backend laundering

An LLM may:

- compute the decisive result once in a shared Python helper;
- convert it to a JAX array and a PyTorch tensor;
- reproduce the same authored table in Julia;
- call those three “independent engines”;
- and hash all artifacts correctly.

Hashes would prove stable laundering, not independent computation.

### 3.2 Decorative native work

A real JAX, Julia, or PyTorch operation may run while being causally irrelevant to the claimed observable. Examples include:

- counting already-authored Boolean rows;
- differentiating a trivial margin unrelated to the scientific mechanism;
- allocating a device array;
- compiling a constant emitter;
- solving an SMT formula whose decisive truth values were inserted as constants;
- invoking a library only to print its version.

### 3.3 Universal-evidence gaming

If a schema requires all backends and LevOS for all claims, an LLM can satisfy form rather than meaning:

- a no-op Julia command for a JAX-only GPU search;
- a NumPy conversion called an independent engine;
- a PyTorch tensor allocation called a third seal;
- a harmless LevOS echo beside a direct-shell scientific computation;
- irrelevant Git metadata attached to a semantic decision.

### 3.4 Capability drift and stale green status

An engine can remain marked “integrated” after:

- a package update changes semantics;
- a solver default changes;
- a GPU driver changes reduction behavior;
- an environment disappears;
- an arrow implementation is replaced;
- its negative controls stop running;
- a CI job starts skipping the Julia lane;
- the owner contract changes.

### 3.5 Fallback and dependency smuggling

JAX may silently fall back to a different path. Julia may call Python. A library may call NumPy internally. A cached or precomputed `.npy`, Arrow, or JSON artifact may contain the answer. A failed GPU launch may rerun on CPU while preserving a GPU label.

### 3.6 Test authorship closure

If the same LLM writes:

- the implementation;
- the fixture;
- the expected output;
- the tolerance;
- the hostile mutation;
- the validator;
- and the pass field,

then different processes can still form one closed authored loop. The control plane must separate policy, challenge, producer, and verifier authorities.

### 3.7 Semantic substitution

Stable names can hide different objects:

- the owner's 16×4 candidate cells become four sequential substages;
- MSS becomes Pareto non-domination;
- Axis 0 becomes a late telemetry vector;
- a linear QIT ladder is called the manifold;
- a bounded density-matrix analogy is named after a Millennium problem;
- the existence of a Weyl label is treated as chirality;
- an unreset eight-stage cycle is treated as proof of literal 720-degree spinor topology.

### 3.8 Maintenance gaming

An LLM may report “up to date” because:

- the package imported;
- the newest version number was observed;
- only security metadata was refreshed;
- a candidate environment passed a smoke test;
- one library was upgraded while its cross-language peer was not;
- it modified the stable environment in place and then rewrote the lockfile.

“Latest,” “installed,” “healthy,” “accepted,” “integrated,” and “scientifically validated” must remain separate states.

## 4. Trust topology

The proposed control topology uses independently bound components:

```text
owner checksum + policy trust root
              │
              ▼
      ClaimGate route broker
              │
      frozen run ticket/nonce
              │
       ┌──────┴─────────────────────────────────────────┐
       │                                                │
       ▼                                                ▼
independent compute lanes                      optional LevOS host path
Julia / JAX / licensed support                 external bridge only
       │                                                │
separate child artifacts                       host/process/event receipt
       └───────────────────┬────────────────────────────┘
                           ▼
              independent evidence checkers
                           │
                    Ratchet settlement
                           │
              ClaimGate final bounded admission
                           │
           admitted artifact within claim ceiling
```

This drawing does not decide `OD-12`. Operationally, the Sim Engine independence lane and ClaimGate/LevOS control lane should mature in parallel and meet at a governed exact-small transaction. Neither may inherit trust from the other.

### 4.1 Claim admission is not operating-system prevention

ClaimGate can be built at several materially different enforcement strengths. They must never share one undifferentiated “integrated” label.

| Enforcement level | What it can establish | What it cannot establish |
|---|---|---|
| `E0_RECORD_CHECKER` | an artifact or receipt fails a declared admission policy | that the LLM was unable to run another process or publish another artifact elsewhere |
| `E1_ROUTE_BROKER` | compliant jobs receive tickets and unbrokered artifacts are refused by downstream admission | that the LLM could not bypass the broker through shell, network, credentials, or another output path |
| `E2_SUPERVISED_EXECUTION` | the ticketed child tree, artifacts, resources, and declared side effects were captured | that another capability outside the captured tree was technically unavailable |
| `E3_CAPABILITY_CONFINED_RUNTIME` | the model/runtime possesses only broker-mediated executable, filesystem, network, credential, and device capabilities | that LevOS supplied the confinement, unless the LevOS causal host contract also passes |
| `E4_LEVOS_MEDIATED_RUNTIME` | the declared capability was causally mediated through an attested LevOS path and the protected checkout was unchanged | a future/full ClaimGate operating system exists, or that unrelated machine paths were confined |

At `E0` or `E1`, ClaimGate can reject evidence produced outside the deterministic route, which is valuable, but it cannot truthfully say it prevented the bypass from occurring. True prevention requires an OS-enforced or equivalently capability-confined runtime:

- the LLM process has no direct shell or arbitrary process-launch capability;
- relevant executables are available only through the broker;
- filesystem write roots are denied except ticket-scoped outputs;
- network destinations and credentials are absent or broker-scoped;
- GPU/device access is granted only to the sealed child;
- unbrokered output cannot enter the trusted artifact store;
- detached children and alternate IPC paths are denied or observed;
- the policy and broker live outside candidate-controlled roots.

The required negative test is not merely “ClaimGate rejected an untrusted receipt.” It must attempt a direct shell, alternate interpreter, child detachment, undeclared network call, credential reuse, and direct artifact-store write from inside the purportedly confined model runtime. At `E3`, those actions must be technically denied. Until that evidence exists, reports must say **admission control** or **supervised execution**, not “LLM bypass was prevented.”

This distinction supplies an honest staged route toward the owner's long-term goal that deterministic constraints surround LLM execution:

```text
artifact admission
  → mandatory route broker
  → supervised child execution
  → capability-confined model runtime
  → attested LevOS-mediated capability classes
  → broader OS integration, only after bounded evidence
```

Moving right requires fresh hostile evidence. A later label cannot be inferred from an earlier checkpoint.

### 4.2 Authorities

| Authority | May do | Must not do |
|---|---|---|
| Owner checksum | bind owner-stated semantics, branch statuses, and claim ceilings | be silently reconstructed from repo majority or generated prose |
| Policy trust root | define route rules, role restrictions, maintenance windows, and verifier identities | be writable by the candidate or LLM run |
| LLM/council proposer | propose code, experiments, dependency candidates, and repairs | set its own evidence floor, pass status, or promotion |
| Capability broker | select already licensed lanes from a claim type and arrow graph | accept candidate-authored role labels without registry checks |
| Compute runner | execute one frozen child ticket | alter policy, expected values, or another lane's artifacts |
| Evidence checker | verify bytes, lineage, native execution, and contract predicates | import the producer's decisive result constructor |
| Scientific Ratchet | compare validated candidates under owner-bound constraints and preserve plurality | certify itself or replace MSS with a convenience ranking |
| ClaimGate final checker | admit only the bounded claim supported by all required evidence | declare unrestricted scientific truth |
| LevOS bridge | mediate declared host capabilities outside the protected checkout | edit LevOS, fall back to direct shell, or attest a no-op as decisive mediation |

## 5. Three separate matrices prevent fake “all engines used”

### 5.1 Claim applicability matrix

For every claim type, the policy declares which lanes are:

- `REQUIRED_GENERATOR`;
- `REQUIRED_REFERENCE`;
- `REQUIRED_ADMISSION_CHECK`;
- `OPTIONAL_SUPPORT`;
- `FORBIDDEN`;
- `NOT_APPLICABLE`.

Only policy can assign these states. A producer may request a lane but cannot downgrade a requirement or upgrade an optional lane.

Illustrative policy, subject to owner approval:

| Claim type | Julia | JAX | NumPy | PyTorch | formal lane | LevOS |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| owner semantic decision | `N/A` | `N/A` | `FORBIDDEN` as authority | `FORBIDDEN` as authority | optional consistency only | `N/A` |
| exact-small numerical arrow conformance | required independent reference | required independent workhorse | optional transparent enumerator; poison during independence control | `N/A` unless named task | optional exact obligation | `N/A` |
| large JAX CPU/GPU search | current reference anchor on exact-small or declared sample | required generator | optional inspection only | optional named support task | required if claim needs a certificate | only if host mediation is claimed/required |
| PyTorch training/perception result | reference or baseline only if contract calls for it | comparison baseline where relevant | data inspection only | required generator after justification gate | optional | only if host mediation is claimed/required |
| exact symbolic/rational result | primary where Julia capability is selected | `N/A` unless numerical corroboration | optional transparent check | `N/A` | required checker where claim type demands | `N/A` |
| proof-kernel certificate | optional producer/reference | optional candidate search | `FORBIDDEN` as proof | `FORBIDDEN` as proof | required independent kernel | `N/A` |
| LevOS-hosted action | only if action contains Julia arrow | only if action contains JAX arrow | role per arrow | role per arrow | role per claim | required live causal mediation |

This is not a universal backend table. It is a starting policy template.

### 5.2 Fleet maintenance matrix

Every capability advertised as active must have recurring evidence independent of current campaign selection:

| Registered object | Required recurring evidence |
|---|---|
| stable Julia estate | native exact-small canary, lock integrity, method/package invocation, no hidden Python execution |
| stable JAX estate | x64 native canary, compiled IR, device identity, eager/JIT parity, no fallback |
| stable NumPy satellite | allowlisted diagnostic/enumeration fixture plus poison controls proving it is not load bearing on restricted arrows |
| stable PyTorch task capability | native task canary, dtype/device, held-out metric, ablation, and continuing task-specific justification |
| accepted library API | positive, negative, semantic mutation, dependency kill, and output-schema fixture |
| integrated Julia/JAX arrow | pairwise conformance, asymmetric mutations, error-budget check, and serialization round trip |
| GPU capability | CPU/GPU parity fixture, forced-CPU hostile control, device/driver/container receipt |
| formal checker | SAT/UNSAT or proof fixture, semantic mutation, demand erasure, timeout/unknown controls |
| LevOS capability | harmless live transaction, no-op/direct-shell negative, event binding, and protected-tree equality |

If recurring evidence expires, the object becomes `STALE` or `QUARANTINED`. It does not remain “integrated” because an old report says so.

### 5.3 Campaign integration matrix

Each campaign declares an arrow graph. Each arrow points to:

- its semantic contract;
- current owner branch ID;
- independent implementation IDs;
- accepted library capabilities;
- current conformance receipt;
- required exact/formal checks;
- error budget;
- deletion/mutation tests;
- claim ceiling.

The campaign graph is complete only when every load-bearing edge resolves to current, non-quarantined capabilities. A green fleet dashboard cannot fill a missing campaign edge.

## 6. Required registries

These are proposed policy objects. Their schemas must be strict, content-addressed, and controlled outside candidate write roots.

### 6.1 Owner authority registry

Records:

- owner checksum ID and digest;
- owner-locked statements;
- current defaults;
- open branches and branch IDs;
- historical/superseded aliases;
- forbidden semantic substitutions;
- exact engine schedule and 16×4 checksum;
- claim ceilings.

No repository test or LLM vote may update this registry.

### 6.2 Engine estate registry

One record per independently managed environment:

```yaml
estate:
  estate_id: "julia-reference-macos-arm64-..."
  lane_role: "REFERENCE"
  channel: "STABLE | CANDIDATE | QUARANTINE | RETIRED"
  platform: "..."
  executable_digest: "sha256:..."
  environment_lock_digests: []
  adapter_source_digest: "sha256:..."
  approved_capabilities: []
  forbidden_fallbacks: []
  last_native_canary_receipt: "sha256:..."
  last_independence_receipt: "sha256:..."
  evidence_expiry: "..."
  quarantine_reasons: []
```

An estate is a specific executable/environment/platform tuple. “JAX installed” is not an estate identity.

### 6.3 Library capability registry

One record per named API and task:

- exact package/version/source/channel;
- environment and lock digest;
- API surface;
- typed input/output;
- semantic assumptions introduced by the library;
- isolated positive and negative fixtures;
- semantic mutation;
- dependency kill;
- known numerical behavior;
- platform matrix;
- license/advisory state;
- current channel;
- last acceptance receipt.

A package may have one accepted API and another untested API.

### 6.4 Arrow registry

One record per mathematical arrow:

- owner branch and semantic schema;
- domain, codomain, carrier, units, and basis;
- exact map or operational contract;
- all introduced primitives or assumptions;
- Julia implementation ID;
- JAX implementation ID;
- optional support implementations;
- exact-small fixture source;
- tolerance/error relation declared before execution;
- metamorphic properties;
- deletion effect;
- negative controls;
- pairwise comparison rule;
- current integration state.

The Julia and JAX implementation source graphs may share a prose mathematical specification and serialization schema. They may not share executable result constructors, expected tables, hidden helper code, or foreign-function calls that decide the observable.

### 6.5 Campaign registry

Records:

- campaign and generation ID;
- native scientific statement;
- bounded fixture statement;
- owner branches used;
- arrow graph;
- standard baselines;
- serious negative branches;
- resource bounds;
- stop rules;
- requested claim ceiling;
- formal/lift seam;
- current maturity.

A Millennium-problem name does not grant a campaign any theorem-level tier.

### 6.6 Policy and verifier registry

Records:

- policy digest;
- trusted parser and checker digests;
- allowed state transitions;
- claim applicability matrix;
- maintenance cadence;
- challenge-provider identities;
- approved runner identities;
- protected roots;
- allowed output roots;
- maximum resource limits;
- revocation list.

Candidate code cannot carry its own trusted policy copy.

## 7. Capability broker

The proposed capability broker turns a frozen claim contract and campaign graph into a route plan.

### 7.1 Inputs

- owner checksum digest;
- policy digest;
- claim type and ceiling;
- campaign/arrow graph digest;
- platform/resource request;
- current estate snapshot;
- current quarantine and revocation list.

### 7.2 Resolution rules

1. Resolve every arrow by exact registry ID, never a free-text backend name.
2. Derive required lanes from policy and arrow contracts.
3. Reject an inactive, stale, candidate-only, or quarantined capability for a stable major run.
4. Reject role inversion, such as NumPy becoming the sole generator for a workhorse arrow.
5. Require a current Julia/JAX conformance receipt for each integrated numerical arrow.
6. Require full same-instance execution in both lanes when the claim itself is cross-engine agreement.
7. Permit a large JAX run with a declared Julia reference anchor only when the claim is not misrepresented as whole-instance cross-engine agreement.
8. Require a task-specific PyTorch license. Global “PyTorch available” is insufficient.
9. Mark irrelevant lanes `NOT_APPLICABLE`; never synthesize dummy receipts.
10. Require LevOS only when the selected route is host-mediated or the claim explicitly includes LevOS integration.
11. Emit a deterministic route plan or fail. The LLM cannot hand-edit the resolved plan.

### 7.3 Route completeness

The broker computes a graph of required evidence, not a backend count:

```text
scientific claim
  ├─ generated-by → JAX child run A
  ├─ reference-anchored-by → Julia child run B
  ├─ compared-by → independent conformance checker C
  ├─ certified-by → proof/interval child D
  └─ host-mediated-by → LevOS child E, only when applicable
```

Every final claim field must trace through this graph to the process and input bytes that produced it.

## 8. Run ticket and nonce

No major Codex-Ratchet run may begin from an ad hoc shell prompt. ClaimGate should issue a frozen, single-generation run ticket.

### 8.1 Ticket contents

- ticket schema and version;
- random transaction nonce generated outside the candidate process;
- generation ID;
- owner checksum and open-branch IDs;
- policy and registry snapshot digests;
- campaign and arrow graph digests;
- exact input artifact digests;
- selected estates and capabilities;
- per-child executable/environment/source digests;
- expected semantic outputs, but not producer-authored answer values;
- predeclared tolerances and error budgets;
- allowed process tree;
- allowed environment variables;
- allowed filesystem and network scopes;
- resource ceilings and deadlines;
- required hostile controls;
- output directory and artifact names;
- final claim ceiling;
- LevOS applicability status.

### 8.2 Nonce binding

The nonce must appear in:

- child launch authorization;
- process environment or sealed input;
- temporary artifact namespace;
- native child receipt;
- finalized output metadata;
- comparison receipt;
- host event when LevOS is applicable;
- final ClaimGate receipt.

The nonce must not be used as the scientific answer. It binds one execution history and prevents stale artifact replay.

### 8.3 Ticket immutability

After Seal 1 or its equivalent route checkpoint:

- changes to code, dependencies, inputs, tolerances, seed policy, resource limits, or branch IDs create a new generation;
- a timeout cannot be resumed under the old ticket after modifying settings;
- a failed run cannot overwrite its generation;
- a repaired candidate re-enters as a new ticket linked to the failed parent.

## 9. Proposed ClaimGate checkpoints

Audit Round 1 correctly noted that a “two-seal ClaimGate” is a useful proposed topology, not yet owner-locked system identity. This design uses two checkpoints as a control candidate.

### 9.1 Pre-run route seal

Checks:

- strict raw parsing before ordinary JSON object construction;
- duplicate-key, Unicode-confusable, size, and finite-value rejection;
- owner checksum and policy digest;
- claim-type applicability;
- nonempty claim-specific requirements;
- current estate and quarantine status;
- exact code, lock, input, and campaign digests;
- no role inversion;
- fresh generation and nonce;
- protected and allowed roots;
- required hostile-control plan;
- resource feasibility;
- no unresolved semantic substitution.

Outputs:

- `ROUTE_SEALED`;
- `PENDING_REQUIRED_EVIDENCE`;
- `PARKED_OWNER_DECISION`;
- `BLOCKED_INVALID`.

It never emits scientific `PASS`.

### 9.2 Post-run admission seal

Checks:

- all required child receipts are present and independently parsed;
- child outputs bind to ticket nonce and exact inputs;
- chronology is possible and monotonic;
- native witnesses correspond to the executable that produced each artifact;
- required lanes performed load-bearing or admission-bearing roles;
- comparison was independently recomputed;
- hostile controls produced their predeclared effects;
- no undeclared engine, fallback, or detached child appears;
- error budgets remain as predeclared;
- formal `UNKNOWN`, timeout, OOM, nonfinite values, or disagreement are not passes;
- no protected root changed;
- Ratchet settlement uses the owner branch and preserves plural survivors;
- final wording stays within the earned claim ceiling.

Outputs:

- `ADMITTED_WITHIN_CEILING`;
- `PENDING_EXTERNAL_CHECK`;
- `PARKED_INCOMPARABLE`;
- `BLOCKED`;
- `QUARANTINED_FLEET_DEFECT`.

## 10. What proves an engine was genuinely used

Backend presence is insufficient. This design distinguishes three causal roles.

### 10.1 Generating load-bearing

The lane's native execution produces the scientific observable.

Evidence:

- output bytes were created by a descendant of the declared executable;
- native IR or method witness includes the mechanism-specific operations;
- output changes under a predeclared semantic mutation;
- output does not exist or is invalid if that lane is deleted;
- the lane does not import or read another lane's answer;
- provenance timestamps and hashes bind input → executable → output.

### 10.2 Admission load-bearing

The lane independently checks or references the generated result. Deleting it may not change the JAX numerical value, but must prevent final admission.

Example: a Julia exact-small reference lane anchors a large JAX GPU search. The Julia child is admission-bearing, not falsely described as generating the full GPU result.

Evidence:

- independent semantics and source;
- selected shared instances or analytic invariants declared before execution;
- disagreement blocks;
- mutation of the checker/reference causes a predicted disagreement or failure;
- the producer cannot forge the comparison receipt.

### 10.3 Support-only

The lane assists but cannot support the claim alone.

Examples:

- NumPy converts or inspects a finalized artifact;
- PyTorch trains an optional perception candidate;
- SymPy proposes a simplified identity;
- PySINDy proposes a candidate AST.

The receipt must state exactly what disappears if the support lane is removed. A support operation may not be promoted by calling it “engine evidence.”

## 11. Native evidence requirements by lane

### 11.1 Julia reference

Minimum child receipt:

- executable absolute identity and digest;
- Julia version and platform;
- `Project.toml` and `Manifest.toml` digests;
- package and named solver versions;
- source-tree digest;
- declared module/method identity;
- lowered-code or method-dispatch witness where useful;
- process tree showing no hidden Python, JAX, or answer-producing subprocess;
- input and output digests;
- semantic mutation result;
- dependency-kill result;
- exact/rational versus floating mode;
- error enclosure where numerical;
- peak memory and timing.

Hostile controls:

- break the selected Julia package and require Julia failure without affecting JAX;
- change a load-bearing Julia kernel and require Julia-only output change;
- plant a Python fallback and require process-lineage rejection;
- feed an answer table from JAX and require independence rejection.

### 11.2 JAX workhorse

Minimum child receipt:

- Python, JAX, and JAXLIB versions and lock digest;
- `jax_enable_x64` state;
- platform and actual device identity;
- source digest;
- JAXPR or StableHLO digest;
- compile and execution times;
- input/output digests;
- evidence that output depends on the compiled mechanism;
- eager/JIT relation where required;
- deterministic or statistical seed policy;
- reduction-order and tolerance policy;
- no NumPy result-producing fallback;
- peak host/device memory.

On the owner's M1, the host ISA is `arm64`. JAX's `jax_enable_x64` flag denotes 64-bit numerical types, not an `x86_64` host. Receipts must state both properties separately.

Hostile controls:

- mutate the native JAX kernel and require JAX-only output change;
- kill JAX or a selected library and require hard failure;
- force CPU while claiming GPU and require rejection;
- return a constant from the compiled function and require hidden challenge failure;
- poison NumPy result-producing paths;
- replay an old compiled artifact under a new ticket and require nonce rejection.

### 11.3 NumPy satellite

NumPy is not made trustworthy by banning all imports. It is controlled by typed allowlists.

Permitted receipts identify:

- exact-small enumeration;
- transparent baseline;
- artifact conversion;
- inspection or diagnostic statistic;
- serialization boundary.

Controls:

- poison or trap result-producing NumPy calls during Julia/JAX independence tests;
- distinguish conversion from computation;
- scan for `.npy` or other precomputed answer tables;
- inspect transitive SciPy and library use;
- remove NumPy and require authoritative Julia/JAX arrows to remain unchanged;
- if a permitted exact-small NumPy oracle is used, independently derive its expected result and never call it a third scientific engine.

### 11.4 PyTorch support/training

Each accepted task requires:

- named task and why Julia/JAX is insufficient or materially weaker;
- model, optimizer, dtype, device, seed, data split, and checkpoint digests;
- held-out metric and standard baseline;
- ablation proving the PyTorch-specific component contributes;
- training/test leakage controls;
- metadata-stripping controls for perception;
- native graph/operation witness;
- resource cost;
- explicit statement that the task does not replace reference semantics.

If the advantage disappears after an update, the task capability returns to `CANDIDATE` or `QUARANTINE`.

### 11.5 GPU execution

Required:

- exact GPU UUID/model;
- driver, CUDA, relevant library, and container digests;
- JAX/IR/native-kernel witness;
- actual device memory high-water mark;
- local CPU golden fixture in declared 64-bit numerical mode;
- declared CPU/GPU parity relation;
- forced-CPU hostile control;
- reproducibility/statistical policy;
- provider, duration, and cost receipt;
- no claim above the bounded computation or certificate actually checked.

## 12. Semantic independence and pairwise conformance

### 12.1 Independence is not implementation diversity alone

Julia and JAX may agree because both are correct, or because both copied the same mistake. The required evidence combines:

- different decisive source implementations;
- different dependency graphs;
- different native execution;
- independently derived exact-small fixtures or external known results;
- asymmetric mutation;
- dependency kill;
- metamorphic properties;
- serious negatives;
- hidden challenges supplied after implementation freeze.

### 12.2 Required pairwise matrix for each active numerical arrow

| Test | Julia expectation | JAX expectation | Admission effect |
|---|---|---|---|
| declared exact-small fixture | computes reference | computes workhorse output | compare under predeclared relation |
| Julia-only semantic mutation | changes/fails | unchanged | test must detect divergence |
| JAX-only semantic mutation | unchanged | changes/fails | test must detect divergence |
| Julia dependency kill | hard fail | unchanged | no Julia fallback |
| JAX dependency kill | unchanged | hard fail | no JAX fallback |
| NumPy poison | unchanged unless explicitly satellite-scoped | unchanged | hidden common path blocks |
| shared expected-table injection | reject | reject | independence blocks |
| corrupt serialized input | reject | reject | schema/atomicity blocks |
| basis/unit mutation | predictable transform or reject | same contract | silent basis coercion blocks |
| hidden challenge | derived independently | derived independently | constant emitters block |

### 12.3 Comparison receipt

The comparison process:

- runs in a third process;
- receives finalized Julia and JAX artifacts;
- cannot import either result constructor;
- recomputes canonicalization, units, shapes, and the comparison metric;
- applies the predeclared error decomposition;
- records incomparable/disagreement states without averaging them away;
- binds both child receipt IDs and the run ticket;
- cannot alter either child artifact.

### 12.4 Error decomposition

Agreement must not be reduced to one adjustable tolerance. Record separately:

- representation;
- rounding;
- solver;
- discretization/truncation;
- sampling;
- projection/rationalization;
- serialization;
- model-form uncertainty.

Tolerance changes after seeing a result create a new generation and invalidate the prior route seal.

## 13. Fleet coverage: making every active lane run regularly without faking every claim

### 13.1 Coverage rules

1. Every `STABLE` estate runs a native canary on schedule.
2. Every `STABLE` library capability runs its isolated positive and negative fixtures on schedule.
3. Every active numerical arrow has a current Julia/JAX conformance receipt.
4. Every arrow rotates through asymmetric mutation and dependency-kill controls.
5. Every major run refreshes the exact capabilities it will use, regardless of routine schedule.
6. NumPy poison controls run against every arrow that forbids NumPy as load bearing.
7. PyTorch is called integrated only for named tasks with current justification evidence.
8. A lane with no approved task is `AVAILABLE` or `CANDIDATE`, not globally integrated.
9. A skipped scheduled job creates coverage debt and then staleness; it never inherits green.
10. Coverage metrics report evidence presence and expiry. They do not measure scientific truth.

### 13.2 Evidence freshness windows

Exact windows should be owner/policy decisions. A proposed default:

- native estate canary: 24 hours;
- isolated library acceptance subset: 7 days;
- active arrow conformance: 7 days;
- asymmetric mutation/dependency-kill rotation: 7 days, with full matrix monthly;
- GPU parity: per driver/container change and within 30 days of a major GPU run;
- LevOS harmless host fixture: per bridge/LevOS/policy change and within 7 days of a C7 run;
- full clean rebuild: monthly and before a release;
- full hostile suite: release and before a major scientific run.

Expiry does not prove a defect. It changes status to `STALE`, which prevents use until refreshed.

### 13.3 Coverage debt

Track typed debts, not a single health percentage:

- missing native canary;
- missing negative;
- missing semantic mutation;
- missing dependency kill;
- missing cross-language parity;
- unreviewed security advisory;
- stale lockfile snapshot;
- missing platform check;
- missing task justification;
- stale host attestation.

No weighted score may let ten cheap canaries cancel one missing load-bearing independence test.

Each component and each named library API needs its **own** exercise-debt record. A top-level green Julia or JAX estate must not hide an unused package.

```yaml
exercise_debt:
  object_id: "exact estate, library capability, adapter, or arrow ID"
  object_kind: "ESTATE | LIBRARY_API | ADAPTER | ARROW"
  last_native_positive: "receipt or null"
  last_independent_negative: "receipt or null"
  last_semantic_mutation: "receipt or null"
  last_dependency_kill: "receipt or null"
  last_serialization_roundtrip: "receipt or null"
  last_arrow_integration_use: "receipt or null"
  last_campaign_use: "receipt or null"
  platforms_exercised: []
  currently_required_platforms: []
  overdue_obligations: []
  resulting_state: "..."
```

Four statuses must remain distinct:

1. `AVAILABLE`: present or buildable;
2. `ISOLATED_ACCEPTED`: its named API passed a bounded capability fixture;
3. `ARROW_INTEGRATED`: it causally performs a declared operation in a registered arrow;
4. `CAMPAIGN_EXERCISED`: that integrated arrow was actually used in a sealed campaign generation.

An active library must have current isolated evidence and, if called integrated, current arrow-use evidence. A library advertised as campaign-active must have a current campaign receipt. If it has not been needed by recent claims, the system should demote the description to `AVAILABLE_STABLE` or `ISOLATED_ACCEPTED`, not insert a dummy call into an unrelated claim. This gives every component honest recurring exercise while preserving claim-typed applicability.

The maintenance scheduler should select due fixtures from this per-object debt graph. It must not choose the cheapest test repeatedly, and it must report overdue obligations by name rather than compressing them into a fleet-health percentage.

### 13.4 Library-level exercise proof

For a library to count as genuinely exercised, its receipt must show:

- the exact named API was invoked;
- the API's output causally affected the fixture observable;
- deleting or semantically mutating that API changed or blocked the fixture as predicted;
- the fixture was not satisfied by a cached table or fallback;
- positive and negative controls used independently sourced expectations;
- the process ran in the registered estate and platform;
- the library did not silently cross into a forbidden lane;
- the evidence is within the policy freshness window.

Version-printing, imports, object construction, and conversions do not pay exercise debt.

### 13.5 Hidden challenge custody

At least one challenge family should be controlled outside the implementation author:

- challenge fixtures are generated or selected after code freeze;
- expected outputs are kept from the producer where practical;
- the verifier reveals only bounded residuals needed for repair;
- every challenge has a provenance and semantic contract;
- challenges are rotated;
- failure is retained without being treated as proof against the owner's model.

An LLM must not author both the hidden challenge and the candidate in one context.

## 14. Stable, candidate, quarantine, stale, and retired channels

Status attaches separately to estates, libraries, arrows, and campaigns.

### 14.1 State meanings

| State | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `DISCOVERED` | observed in source or environment; not accepted |
| `CANDIDATE` | isolated sandbox exists; acceptance incomplete |
| `STABLE` | pinned object passed its required scope-specific gates |
| `STALE` | evidence expired or environment drifted; no known scientific failure, but use is blocked |
| `QUARANTINED` | a concrete defect, bypass, advisory, disagreement, or trust violation exists |
| `RETIRED` | intentionally removed from active routing; preserved as provenance/negative fixture |

### 14.2 No implicit promotion

- Import success promotes nothing.
- A version update returns the object to `CANDIDATE`.
- A successful candidate canary does not promote an arrow.
- A successful arrow fixture does not promote a campaign.
- A successful campaign does not promote a theorem.
- An LLM recommendation cannot promote any object.
- An emergency action may auto-quarantine a component. It may not auto-promote a replacement.

### 14.3 Quarantine propagation

Propagation follows declared dependency edges:

- quarantined estate blocks capabilities in that estate;
- quarantined library capability blocks arrows using that exact API;
- quarantined arrow blocks campaign routes using it;
- unrelated estates and arrows remain available;
- the system does not mark the whole project failed unless the dependency graph requires it.

This prevents both fail-open behavior and narrative over-collapse.

## 15. Dependency update procedure

“Regularly up to date” means **regularly observed, assessed, tested, and deliberately promoted**, not automatically moved to the newest version.

### 15.1 Read-only discovery

On schedule:

- query official package and security-advisory sources;
- record retrieval time and source;
- compare available versions to stable pins;
- classify security, compatibility, performance, and feature relevance;
- do not alter the stable environment.

An LLM may summarize update candidates. It may not decide that “latest” is safe.

### 15.2 Candidate environment

For each proposed update:

1. clone or construct a clean candidate environment outside stable roots;
2. pin exact dependencies and transitive locks;
3. generate a software bill of materials;
4. record API and behavior diffs;
5. rerun the named library acceptance suite;
6. rerun every active arrow using the changed dependency;
7. run semantic mutations and dependency kills;
8. run cross-language serialization and conformance;
9. compare error, performance, memory, and determinism;
10. run philosophy/semantic assumption diff;
11. produce a promotion request;
12. leave stable untouched until independent approval.

### 15.3 Promotion

Promotion requires:

- all scope-relevant acceptance tests;
- no unexplained numerical or semantic drift;
- compatible Julia/JAX pairwise behavior;
- updated native witnesses;
- updated lock and registry digests;
- rollback artifact;
- policy-authorized approval outside the proposer;
- a new stable generation.

The previous stable environment remains content-addressed for rollback and reproduction.

### 15.4 Emergency security response

For a critical issue:

- quarantine the affected capability immediately;
- block new runs that depend on it;
- allow only forensic/reproduction access under a separate route;
- test an updated candidate or disable the capability;
- do not silently fall back to NumPy, CPU, another solver, or an old environment;
- do not reinterpret missing capacity as a scientific negative.

### 15.5 Freeze before major runs

A major run should use a predeclared freeze window:

- no routine dependency changes after preflight;
- urgent changes invalidate the ticket and require a new preflight;
- stable and candidate environments never share mutable package caches if that can change behavior;
- cloud images are selected by digest, not floating tag.

## 16. Maintenance cadence

These are proposed defaults, not evidence that any scheduler exists.

### 16.1 Before every execution

- validate ticket, nonce, policy, owner checksum, and registry snapshot;
- verify executable and lock digests;
- verify selected capability status and evidence freshness;
- run a cheap mechanism-specific native canary;
- verify allowed roots and resource limits;
- check for stale or pre-existing output;
- ensure no prohibited fallback is available or silently selected;
- validate input schema, units, basis, finite values, and generation;
- bind process supervision.

### 16.2 Daily

- executable and lock integrity;
- one native canary per stable estate;
- environment/device identity;
- quarantine/revocation synchronization;
- disk, memory, and output-root health;
- scheduler skip detection;
- no stale child processes;
- no stable-root mutation;
- read-only advisory discovery.

Daily canaries establish availability, not scientific correctness.

### 16.3 Weekly

- active library positive/negative fixtures;
- active arrow Julia/JAX conformance;
- rotating asymmetric mutation and dependency-kill tests;
- NumPy poison tests;
- cross-process serialization corruption tests;
- JAX eager/JIT and CPU 64-bit numerical-mode checks;
- PyTorch named-task held-out check where applicable;
- formal checker mutation/unknown/timeout tests;
- ClaimGate hostile parser suite;
- coverage-debt report.

### 16.4 Monthly

- clean rebuild of every stable estate;
- full independence matrix;
- all accepted library capability tests;
- complete cross-language round trips;
- dependency/version/advisory review;
- candidate update bakeoff;
- resource/performance/error trend review;
- policy and registry integrity audit;
- hidden challenge rotation;
- archived receipt replay;
- rollback rehearsal;
- protected-root and bridge audit.

### 16.5 Release candidate

- owner checksum and v8 content manifest explicitly bound;
- clean-room reconstruction from locks;
- full hostile-control battery;
- all active arrow matrices;
- campaign end-to-end spine;
- no conditional/skipped required lane;
- full parser and receipt-chain tests;
- LevOS harmless and no-op controls if release claims C7;
- CPU/GPU parity if release claims GPU capability;
- external reproduction package;
- rollback and quarantine plan.

### 16.6 After every major run

- terminate and account for all descendants;
- finalize artifacts atomically;
- verify output chronology and ticket binding;
- recompute comparison receipts independently;
- compare protected roots before/after;
- scan for undeclared outputs and network/process effects;
- record resource and error budgets;
- classify failures as scientific, infrastructure, policy, or unknown;
- append immutable lineage;
- expire secrets and cloud resources;
- update coverage without promoting the scientific claim automatically.

## 17. Major-run preflight

A major run is any run that is expensive, cloud/GPU-based, difficult to reproduce, intended to alter active architecture, or likely to support a strong scientific statement.

### 17.1 Preflight gates

#### Authority gate

- exact owner checksum;
- exact v8 content manifest or explicit non-v8 branch;
- open decisions listed;
- no generated document promoted by salience;
- no silent version merge.

#### Semantic gate

- native problem and bounded fixture separated;
- carrier, units, maps, basis, and error model declared;
- introduced primitives enumerated;
- owner engine schedule and 16×4 semantics checked where applicable;
- IGT casing and non-moralization checked where applicable;
- Axis-0 and manifold branch IDs stated;
- no MSS/Pareto substitution;
- claim ceiling frozen.

#### Fleet gate

- selected estates are stable and current;
- active arrows have current conformance;
- exact libraries are accepted;
- no role inversion;
- no unexplained dependency drift;
- clean native canaries pass.

#### Independence gate

- decisive Julia/JAX implementations do not share a result constructor;
- asymmetric mutation evidence is current;
- dependency-kill evidence is current;
- NumPy poison policy is current;
- hidden challenge is available;
- independent comparison checker is available.

#### Resource gate

- dry-run scale and memory estimate;
- wall-time, cost, and storage budget;
- timeout/OOM behavior;
- checkpoint policy that cannot overwrite generations;
- cloud container/device digest;
- stop rules acknowledged.

#### ClaimGate gate

- strict parser and verifier identities;
- read-only policy roots;
- sealed run ticket and nonce;
- output quarantine directory;
- process/environment/filesystem allowlists;
- required hostile controls.

#### LevOS gate, only when applicable

- exact protected checkout identity;
- external bridge digest;
- read-only enforcement;
- current harmless capability fixture;
- authenticated session/event binding;
- pre-run tree manifest;
- no direct-shell fallback.

### 17.2 Preflight result

Only:

- `READY_FOR_SEALED_RUN`;
- `PENDING_EVIDENCE`;
- `PARKED_OWNER_DECISION`;
- `BLOCKED_FLEET_STATE`;
- `BLOCKED_POLICY`.

“Almost ready” is not an execution state.

## 18. Execution stop rules

The supervisor terminates or quarantines a run when any of these occurs:

1. policy, owner checksum, code, lock, input, or ticket digest changes;
2. a required engine is missing, stale, or skipped;
3. an undeclared backend or process appears;
4. Julia calls a hidden Python/JAX result path;
5. JAX falls back or executes on the wrong device;
6. NumPy becomes result producing where forbidden;
7. PyTorch runs without a named licensed task;
8. a native witness is missing or unrelated to the mechanism;
9. output exists before the ticket or nonce;
10. a stale receipt or session/event ID is replayed;
11. comparison is outside the predeclared relation;
12. a tolerance or seed policy changes mid-run;
13. a required hostile control is skipped;
14. solver status is `UNKNOWN`, timeout, resource-out, or malformed;
15. nonfinite values occur outside an explicitly typed symbolic object;
16. an OOM, timeout, detached child, or resource ceiling is reached;
17. an output is truncated or atomically incomplete;
18. a protected root changes or receives a write attempt;
19. LevOS evidence is missing for a host-mediated route;
20. LevOS runs a no-op while the decisive workload executes elsewhere;
21. the scientific object changes identity under a stable label;
22. the claim wording exceeds the frozen ceiling.

### 18.1 Failure typing

| Failure class | Meaning | Purgatory interpretation |
|---|---|---|
| `SCIENTIFIC_DISCRIMINATOR_FAIL` | a valid test rejects or differentiates a candidate | may serve as serious negative evidence within that contract |
| `IMPLEMENTATION_FAIL` | code does not implement its declared map | repair candidate; not evidence against the model |
| `INFRASTRUCTURE_FAIL` | environment, timeout, device, serialization, or dependency failure | administrative failure; never a scientific negative |
| `POLICY_FAIL` | missing evidence, stale generation, forbidden route, or trust violation | blocked/quarantined; never a scientific negative |
| `OWNER_DECISION_OPEN` | the run would decide an unresolved semantic branch | parked, preserving plurality |
| `UNKNOWN` | failure cannot be typed with current evidence | parked; never converted to pass or negative |

Purgatory preserves lineage. Membership is not itself evidence that a mathematical branch is wrong.

## 19. Post-run settlement and admission

### 19.1 Artifact finalization

Each child:

1. writes to a ticket-scoped untrusted temporary path;
2. fsyncs and closes;
3. exits;
4. is checked by another process;
5. is atomically finalized;
6. becomes immutable;
7. is reopened by downstream consumers.

No downstream process trusts stdout or in-memory objects as sole evidence.

### 19.2 Scientific settlement

The Ratchet consumes only validated artifacts and:

- checks hard semantic/structural constraints;
- checks owner engine order and state handoff;
- evaluates branch-specific restrictions/extensions;
- preserves typed Axis-0 components without scalar soup;
- applies deletion, order, and metamorphic witnesses;
- compares alternatives under owner-bound MSS rules;
- retains plural incomparable survivors;
- records serious negatives and administrative failures separately.

The Ratchet receipt is evidence for ClaimGate. It cannot admit itself.

### 19.3 Admission wording

Every final response must separate:

- what mathematical object was evaluated;
- which bounded instance was run;
- which engine generated the result;
- which engine referenced or checked it;
- which hostile controls passed;
- what was not run;
- what remains open;
- the maximum claim tier earned.

No number of bounded runs auto-promotes to a lift lemma, theorem, native physical law, or proof of the owner model.

## 20. LevOS causal mediation and no-op detection

### 20.1 Two legitimate LevOS relations

Do not collapse:

1. **Host-mediated scientific execution:** LevOS causally launches or mediates a selected decisive action.
2. **Post-admission capability exposure:** an already admitted Codex-Ratchet capability is exposed through LevOS.

They require different contracts and receipts. A harmless final LevOS echo cannot prove the first.

### 20.2 External non-mutation boundary

The bridge must be outside LevOS and enforce:

- read-only mount or equivalent permissions;
- redirected caches and temporary files;
- no lockfile or generated-file writes;
- no symlink traversal into the protected checkout;
- process-tree write restrictions;
- pre/post full tree manifests, including declared treatment of ignored/untracked files;
- write-attempt monitoring;
- exact commit/tree/entrypoint identity.

This design does not authorize inspection or mutation of the owner's local LevOS checkout.

### 20.3 Causal no-op test

For a route claiming LevOS mediation:

1. ClaimGate generates an unpredictable nonce after the route is sealed.
2. The bridge passes the nonce through the declared LevOS entrypoint.
3. The LevOS-authenticated event binds the ticket, nonce, capability, and child launch.
4. The decisive worker must be a captured descendant of that launch.
5. A mechanism-specific output commits to the nonce and scientific inputs.
6. The output is rejected if an equivalent direct-shell process creates the same scientific bytes without the authenticated LevOS lineage.
7. A hostile variant invokes LevOS for a harmless no-op while running the decisive action directly. ClaimGate must block it.
8. A hostile variant replays an old LevOS event. Freshness and nonce binding must block it.
9. A hostile variant removes LevOS. The bridge must block rather than fall back.

LevOS appearance is not causal mediation. The decisive artifact must have a verified ancestry through the declared host path.

### 20.4 Claim-typed applicability

If a numerical fixture does not claim or require LevOS:

- the receipt states `LEVOS_APPLICABILITY: NOT_APPLICABLE`;
- no dummy event is created;
- the numerical claim can still be valid within its non-host ceiling;
- it earns no C7/host-integration status.

This prevents the universal-schema defect found in Round 1 while keeping LevOS mandatory where actually claimed.

## 21. Proposed receipt family

No producer-authored `passed: true` is trusted. Status is computed by a registered checker.

### 21.1 Fleet snapshot receipt

Binds:

- registry and policy digests;
- estate states;
- capability states;
- evidence freshness;
- quarantine/revocation entries;
- coverage debts;
- generation time and checker.

### 21.2 Run-ticket receipt

Binds:

- claim contract;
- selected branches and arrows;
- selected estates;
- nonce and generation;
- inputs, sources, locks, policy;
- controls, resources, and claim ceiling.

### 21.3 Native child receipt

Binds:

- executable, environment, device, process tree;
- input/output;
- native IR/method witness;
- timestamps and resource use;
- mutation/dependency control result;
- typed role: generating, admission, or support.

### 21.4 Comparison receipt

Binds:

- child receipt IDs;
- comparison checker;
- units/basis/canonicalization;
- error decomposition;
- result relation;
- disagreement/incomparability without forced scalarization.

### 21.5 Maintenance receipt

Binds:

- cadence class;
- exact objects checked;
- skipped checks;
- positive and negative outcomes;
- coverage debt changes;
- resulting state transitions.

### 21.6 Update-candidate receipt

Binds:

- prior stable object;
- candidate versions and full locks;
- advisory/API/semantic diff;
- all affected tests;
- performance/error changes;
- rollback image;
- promotion authority.

### 21.7 Host receipt

Binds:

- external bridge and policy;
- LevOS checkout identity;
- pre/post tree manifests;
- write-attempt log;
- authenticated session/events;
- ticket/nonce/process lineage;
- demanded observable;
- no-op, replay, and fallback controls.

### 21.8 Final admission receipt

Binds all required receipts and includes:

- computed status;
- exact claim ceiling;
- missing/not-applicable lanes;
- unresolved conflicts;
- no producer authority over promotion.

### 21.9 Content-binding requirements

Fields that look like hashes must be recomputed and bound to canonical bytes. Schemas must reject:

- arbitrary `sha256:` strings;
- duplicate keys;
- unrestricted role or metric strings;
- empty requirement floors;
- impossible chronology;
- output predating input;
- stale generations;
- unknown extras;
- nonfinite numerics;
- producer-authored policy or verdicts.

## 22. State machines

### 22.1 Fleet object

```text
UNKNOWN
  → DISCOVERED
  → CANDIDATE
  → STABLE

STABLE → STALE          when evidence expires or environment drifts
STABLE → QUARANTINED    when a defect or trust violation is found
STALE → CANDIDATE       after refresh begins
QUARANTINED → CANDIDATE only after explicit repair generation
any non-UNKNOWN state → RETIRED by policy
```

There is no direct `DISCOVERED → STABLE`, update-in-place, or self-promotion edge.

### 22.2 Major run

```text
DRAFT
  → ROUTE_ELIGIBLE
  → ROUTE_SEALED
  → PREFLIGHT_READY
  → RUNNING
  → POSTFLIGHT_PENDING
  → SETTLEMENT_PENDING
  → ADMITTED_WITHIN_CEILING
```

From any active state, a typed failure leads to `BLOCKED`, `PARKED`, or `QUARANTINED`. A repaired run starts a new generation; it does not jump backward while keeping the same ticket.

### 22.3 Dependency update

```text
UPDATE_OBSERVED
  → CANDIDATE_ENVIRONMENT
  → ISOLATED_ACCEPTANCE
  → AFFECTED_ARROW_CONFORMANCE
  → HOSTILE_AND_PHILOSOPHY_AUDIT
  → PROMOTION_REQUEST
  → STABLE_NEW_GENERATION
```

Any unexplained difference leads to `PARKED_UPDATE` or `QUARANTINED_UPDATE`, not automatic tolerance expansion.

## 23. Philosophy-conformance gate

Deterministic execution can faithfully compute the wrong ontology. Every major route therefore needs a semantic-assumption audit before numerical execution.

### 23.1 Primitive-introduction ledger

For every arrow and library, list whether it presupposes:

- primitive identity or object individuation;
- equality;
- time or update order;
- causality;
- probability;
- metric, norm, topology, or measure;
- Cartesian coordinates or privileged frame;
- global evaluator/optimizer;
- completed infinity or continuum;
- observer privilege;
- thermodynamic literalism.

The ledger does not automatically reject all such structures. It prevents them from entering unannounced and distinguishes:

- installed finite implementation substrate;
- owner-locked constraint;
- current default;
- open candidate;
- derived/earned structure;
- bridge assumption;
- historical fuel.

### 23.2 Mandatory semantic attack checks

Before a major run, ask and mechanically bind:

1. Did the implementation preinstall the individuals/distinctions it claims to derive?
2. Did it turn MSS into Pareto optimization?
3. Did it linearize a mutually constraining manifold for software convenience?
4. Did it separate entropy from geometry and add geometry later?
5. Did it make density operators the default substrate before a rival-carrier tournament?
6. Did it demote ring-checkerboard/nested shells to visualization?
7. Did it infer chirality from `Left`/`Right` labels?
8. Did it equate unreset continuity with proven 720-degree spinor topology?
9. Did it change the owner engine order?
10. Did it turn four rival bindings into four chronological substages?
11. Did it sum heterogeneous entropies into one score?
12. Did it moralize IGT casing?
13. Did it let FEP, quantum Darwinism, Hopfield models, or familiar thermodynamics become root ontology rather than rival explanatory branches?
14. Did it call an infrastructure failure a negative boundary?
15. Did it rename a bounded analogy after a native major problem?
16. Did it present GPU search, annealing, or formalized finite verification as an unrestricted proof?

Any `YES` requires a named branch, bridge, or correction before routing.

### 23.3 Library ontology firewall

A convenient API must not define the project:

- a density-matrix library does not establish density operators as fundamental;
- an ODE solver does not establish primitive time;
- a graph library does not establish a primitive node identity;
- an optimizer does not establish global optimization as MSS;
- a thermodynamic package does not establish heat/cold as literal meanings of Axes 4/5;
- a Clifford library does not establish physical Weyl chirality;
- a proof solver does not establish that the encoded obligation is the owner's claim.

The arrow registry must state what the library assumes and what experiment could reject that carrier or mapping.

## 24. Acceptance and hostile test battery

These tests are proposed minimum discriminators. Passing them would establish only their bounded control claims.

### 24.1 Fleet and role tests

1. **Role inversion:** mark NumPy as authoritative workhorse; broker must reject.
2. **Fake third engine:** add a PyTorch tensor summary to Julia/JAX outputs; it must remain support/decorative, not independent.
3. **Skipped Julia:** suppress Julia in a required conformance run; final admission must block.
4. **Irrelevant universal lane:** require PyTorch on a pure SMT claim; policy validation must reject the route design rather than demand dummy evidence.
5. **Stale evidence:** expire a native canary; estate becomes `STALE`.
6. **Import-only:** install/import a library without a named fixture; capability remains `DISCOVERED`.
7. **Taskless PyTorch:** run a generic gradient; PyTorch remains unlicensed for scientific use.
8. **Satellite leak:** let a NumPy helper compute the decisive observable; independence blocks.

### 24.2 Independence tests

9. **Shared-kernel laundering:** Julia/JAX wrappers consume one result table; independence blocks.
10. **Julia-only mutation:** only Julia result changes.
11. **JAX-only mutation:** only JAX result changes.
12. **Julia dependency kill:** Julia hard-fails; JAX remains unchanged.
13. **JAX dependency kill:** JAX hard-fails; Julia remains unchanged.
14. **Precomputed answer file:** inject `.npy`/JSON expected output; lineage or hidden challenge blocks.
15. **Constant emitter:** compiled JAX returns a constant; hidden challenge detects it.
16. **Validator imports producer:** comparison process imports result constructor; checker trust blocks.
17. **Tolerance laundering:** widen tolerance after disagreement; generation invalidates.
18. **Basis mismatch:** same numeric array in different basis; semantic comparison blocks.

### 24.3 Native execution tests

19. **JAX CPU masquerades as GPU:** device witness blocks.
20. **Decorative HLO:** HLO lacks mechanism-specific dependency; generating-role claim blocks.
21. **Julia hidden Python:** process tree shows Python child; independence blocks.
22. **Wrong package API:** library version prints but named API is never invoked; capability blocks.
23. **Eager/JIT divergence:** relation outside contract; JAX capability quarantines.
24. **Nonfinite payload:** `NaN`/infinity enters numeric receipt; parser blocks.
25. **Output-before-input:** old output reused under new generation; nonce/chronology blocks.

### 24.4 Receipt and ClaimGate tests

26. **Duplicate key:** raw JSON contains conflicting keys; parse blocks before object creation.
27. **Empty floor:** zero required metrics for a claim needing evidence; route parks/blocks.
28. **Hash-shaped fiction:** arbitrary `sha256:` string; recomputation blocks.
29. **Stale generation:** replay valid prior receipts; nonce blocks.
30. **Producer pass:** child writes `passed:true`; ignored and independently recomputed.
31. **Unknown metric rename:** `acc` becomes `acc_v2`; registry rejects.
32. **Solver unknown:** `UNKNOWN` cannot become pass.
33. **Tautology substitution:** erase scientific demand from SMT obligation; semantic mutation/demand-erasure control blocks.
34. **Truncated artifact:** atomic finalization prevents downstream use.
35. **Detached process:** child escapes supervisor; postflight quarantines.

### 24.5 Update and maintenance tests

36. **In-place update:** mutate stable environment; integrity check quarantines.
37. **Newest-is-best:** newer version passes import but changes arrow semantics; promotion parks.
38. **Advisory only:** metadata refresh without execution; no health promotion.
39. **Skipped scheduler:** required weekly job skipped; coverage debt and staleness appear.
40. **Rollback rehearsal:** candidate promoted in test, then restored to prior stable bytes.
41. **Transitive drift:** unchanged top-level version with changed transitive lock; new generation required.
42. **Platform drift:** local CPU pass is reused for new GPU driver; GPU route blocks pending parity.

### 24.6 LevOS tests

43. **Missing LevOS:** host route blocks; no direct-shell fallback.
44. **LevOS no-op:** LevOS echoes nonce while decisive work runs directly; causal lineage blocks.
45. **Direct shell same bytes:** correct output without authenticated LevOS event; host attestation blocks.
46. **Old event replay:** ticket/nonce freshness blocks.
47. **Protected write:** any LevOS checkout change or write attempt quarantines.
48. **Symlink escape:** output points into protected root; preflight/postflight blocks.
49. **Detached host child:** incomplete process tree blocks.
50. **LevOS not applicable:** pure numerical claim carries typed `NOT_APPLICABLE` and earns no host claim without dummy execution.

### 24.7 Philosophy tests

51. **MSS substitution:** replace MSS with weighted/Pareto score; semantic gate blocks active-default claim.
52. **Linear manifold substitution:** serialize candidate layers and call it owner manifold; branch ID and owner decision required.
53. **16×4 substitution:** execute candidate/measurement/gate/receipt substages; engine identity claim blocks.
54. **Engine order drift:** use historical GitHub order; owner-engine checksum fails.
55. **Chirality by label:** rename branches Left/Right without graded witness; chirality claim blocks.
56. **IGT moralization:** encode WIN as reward/good by default; semantic gate blocks.
57. **Entropy soup:** scalarize typed entropy/geometric components without owner-approved map; gate blocks.
58. **Major-problem namesake:** finite analogy lacks native problem bridge; claim ceiling demotes it.
59. **GPU proof inflation:** finite search claims theorem; admission wording blocks.
60. **Generated authority:** newest/longest manual attempts to replace owner checksum; authority router blocks.

### 24.8 Confinement and per-library debt tests

61. **Admission-versus-prevention wording:** run at `E0` with a valid rejection but an available direct shell; any report saying bypass was technically prevented must fail its claim ceiling.
62. **Capability-confinement escape:** at `E3`, attempt direct shell, alternate interpreter, undeclared network, credential use, detached process, and direct trusted-store write; every path must be technically denied.
63. **Hidden unused library:** leave one named stable library API unexercised while its parent estate canary passes; its own exercise debt must become overdue and its integrated status must expire.
64. **Dummy campaign use:** invoke an otherwise irrelevant library in a campaign without causal effect on an arrow; it must not refresh `last_arrow_integration_use` or `last_campaign_use`.
65. **API-level mutation:** import a package and exercise a non-load-bearing API while the registered API is removed; the capability must fail rather than inherit package-level green.

## 25. Proposed implementation sequence

This is dependency guidance, not a claim that the sequence is owner-approved.

### Parallel foundation A — owner and semantic freeze

- bind an owner checksum and v8 content manifest;
- preserve open branches;
- freeze engine schedule, 16×4 semantics, IGT casing, and compute roles;
- establish the primitive-introduction ledger.

### Parallel foundation B — Sim Engine estate

- perform a read-only inventory;
- identify actual executables, environments, locks, and libraries;
- classify every observed capability as discovered/candidate/stable/quarantined;
- build one exact-small Julia/JAX arrow independently;
- prove asymmetric mutation and dependency-kill behavior.

### Parallel foundation C — ClaimGate trust boundary

- strict raw parser;
- read-only policy root;
- registries and capability broker;
- run-ticket/nonce issuance;
- process and artifact supervision;
- independent receipt checkers.

### Join milestone — one governed exact-small transaction

- route one owner-approved numerical arrow;
- execute Julia and JAX as independent children;
- run NumPy poison;
- compare in a third process;
- perform Ratchet settlement without replacing MSS;
- final bounded admission;
- no LevOS field unless host mediation is actually tested.

### Host milestone — external LevOS harmless and causal fixtures

- protected read-only checkout;
- external bridge;
- harmless event-bound fixture;
- direct-shell and no-op negatives;
- pre/post tree equality;
- separate host receipt.

### Fleet milestone — maintenance scheduler

- daily/weekly/monthly/release plans;
- coverage debt;
- update candidate channel;
- rollback/quarantine;
- no skipped-lane green inheritance.

### Major-run milestone

- campaign-native problem statement;
- clean stable estate;
- full preflight and hostile suite;
- CPU/GPU parity where applicable;
- formal/lift seam;
- claim ceiling enforced.

## 26. Decisions still required

Before this design becomes implementation authority, the owner or protected policy process must decide:

1. the exact owner checksum and v8 content manifest;
2. whether the two-checkpoint ClaimGate topology is accepted or remains one rival;
3. which major-run classes must be ClaimGate-brokered;
4. which classes must additionally be LevOS-mediated;
5. evidence freshness windows;
6. approved exact-small challenge custodians;
7. which Julia and JAX libraries enter the first candidate estate;
8. which arrows are active rather than recovered labels;
9. which PyTorch tasks, if any, have earned support status;
10. exact update and security-response service levels;
11. exact MSS role and its relation to probes/equivalence;
12. manifold, ring, spinor/chirality, Axis-0, and Axes-4/5/6 branch statuses.

Until then, every schema name, cadence interval, state machine, and two-seal flow in this file remains `PROPOSED`.

## 27. Minimum success criterion

The first meaningful success is not “all packages import” or “the full stack is integrated.”

It is one small transaction for which:

- the owner-defined mathematical contract is frozen;
- Julia and JAX independently implement the decisive arrow;
- asymmetric mutations and dependency kills demonstrate independence;
- NumPy is demonstrated non-load-bearing within the declared hostile tests;
- PyTorch is absent unless genuinely required;
- ClaimGate issues and enforces a frozen run ticket;
- every output has causal process/native evidence;
- an independent checker computes the comparison;
- the Ratchet preserves the owner model and plural outcomes;
- final admission stays bounded;
- and, only if a host claim is made, LevOS causally mediates the action without any source mutation.

After that, fleet maintenance can keep the transaction trustworthy while new arrows, libraries, campaigns, and hardware are added one at a time.

## Claim ceiling

This file is a control architecture proposal derived from owner statements and Audit Round 1. It establishes neither:

- the owner's current local Sim Engine estate;
- that any named library is installed or accepted;
- that Julia and JAX currently implement the same arrow independently;
- that NumPy is currently non-load-bearing;
- that any PyTorch task is justified;
- that ClaimGate or its policy root exists as a protected product;
- that LevOS has run or remained unchanged during a live transaction;
- that the scientific Ratchet is executable;
- nor that any mathematical or physical problem has been solved.

Those claims require fresh machine evidence under the controls described here.
